# M10.5: The edit API

Status: **planned** (2026-10-15), not started. Comes between M10 slice 5
and M10 slice 6: M10 paused after slice 5 so that its remaining slices
are built on this API. M10's PR merges to `main` first, and M10.5 is
built from `main`. A few decisions were made while planning (below); the
rest are proposals to settle at kickoff. One commit per slice, with
notes per slice at the end, as in M7–M10.

Research for this plan, all on 2026-10-15:
- sheaf's code at `a0955fc` (M10 slices 1–5 and the pause commit, on
  `m10`);
- a survey by ten readers of the code, the future milestones and prior
  art: model and history, save and materialize, content features, forms,
  the app and CLI, plugins and the agent, rendering, the future
  milestones (M11–M15 and Pending), PDF libraries, and editor
  architectures;
- the M10 worker's notes after slices 3–5 (also at the end of
  [`m10.md`](m10.md));
- measurements of PDFium on a synthetic 7,000-page file and on 402 real
  files of 4 MB or more;
- five competing designs (consumer-first, minimal delivery, model
  definition, object delta, risk-first), judged under three lenses
  (correctness and fit to the future; cleanliness, simplicity and
  deletion; feasibility, performance and delivery) and merged into one;
- a six-lens adversarial review of the merged design: 136 findings, each
  with a ruling, and the accepted ones built in below;
- outside sources, listed at the end.

## Why

**Three mechanisms change what a page shows, and the model sees none of
them as content.**
- **Overlays written at save.** Marks, OCR text layers, the
  header/footer and labels live in `DocState`, and `build_rewrite` or
  the incremental writer draws them into the file. Field values are read
  into the model but not written yet (M10 slice 9); the field tree's
  copies and renames are written by `forms::merge` in both writers.
- **Derived sources.** Crop, watermark/background and Remove
  Header/Footer write a whole new PDF per source to the plaintext cache,
  and `RebasePages` points pages at it. A save then routes the page
  object, `/Annots`, fields and structure parents back to the original
  page (`SourceInfo::derived`, M10 slice 3). The copy exists only to
  change content.
- **Export-only transforms.** Flatten, Clean Copy and print run lopdf
  transforms on the object graph after `build_rewrite`.
- Besides these three, blank pages, images-to-PDF, scans, stamps, tapes,
  the comment summary and Combine build new files outside the model.

What that costs today:
- History, plugins, the planned agent and the save rules can't tell a
  crop from a watermark: `describe.rs` prints "Change the content of …"
  for both (describe.rs:113). `removes_content` treats any page shown
  from another source as removed, so a crop forces a full rewrite though
  it removes nothing, while Remove Header/Footer really does remove.
- Every feature reinvents q/Q isolation and resource merging
  (text_layer.rs, stamps.rs, watermark.rs, save.rs).
- Two writers duplicate the page logic and drift apart: the
  header/footer is drawn only by the rewrite, and the outline is always
  rebuilt by one and only when dirty by the other.
- A derived copy costs O(file) per edit, a PDFium thread, and a
  plaintext file in the cache that is never deleted, form values
  included.
- The screen draws sheaf marks with GPUI, an unsaved header with a GPUI
  overlay, and the rest with PDFium, so the screen and the file can
  disagree.
- Edits aren't pure, so replay is fragile. Created ids aren't returned,
  so the app guesses them. Plans are applied to whatever state is
  current, with no base check. Found while planning has the details.

**What M11–M15 need that today's model can't express:**
- **M11 (security and redaction):** one content path to rewrite (a
  content interpreter on a lexer); the text kept beside content within
  reach (`/SheafAnnot`, bookmark titles, OCR streams); encryption kept
  on every save; no plaintext on disk; a leak check over what is
  written; and "every way out asks first" at one choke point.
- **M12 (signatures):** an incremental append whose bytes are
  reproducible and whose offsets are known; a change classifier for each
  signature permission (DocMDP, FieldMDP, UR3); the file's revisions.
- **M13 (content editing):** stable identity for pages, annotations and
  links across saves, and patches to single keys.
- **M14 (optimize, PDF/A, accessibility):** control over numbering,
  object streams, fonts and the structure tree.
- **M15 (batch) and the agent:** a versioned, serializable edit
  vocabulary, version-checked writes, and a way to revert one turn.

**Goals:**
- One document definition, `DocState`, from which one emitter derives
  every PDF object, on screen and on disk. Page content is part of it.
- One small public vocabulary of semantic ops, used by gestures,
  dialogs, jobs, the CLI and plugins, lowering to one primitive.
- Exact, checked undo with immutable versions, and commits that validate
  what they read.
- Saves as outputs: a planner decides Append or Rewrite from
  content-based effects before a byte is written, and deleting or
  replacing really removes.
- Interop with Acrobat, PDFium and pdf.js; encryption kept; standard PDF
  keys first; nothing on disk except the user's files, the recovery
  sidecar and a short-lived append journal.
- Speed at 7,000 pages: an untouched page costs about 200 bytes, a crop
  of every page commits in under 200 ms, and an incremental save of one
  mark on a 500 MB scan takes under 50 ms.
- Every existing feature re-expressed on the API, and the old paths
  deleted, within the milestone.

## Scope

- **The model:** content-addressed, lazily read sources and a blob store
  (§3.1); `DocState` holding page content, other tools' annotations,
  widgets, the field tree with explicit form instances, the outline and
  generators as records (§3.2–3.9); one coordinate frame below the page
  (§5).
- **The vocabulary:** a new `sheaf-edit` crate of about 25 public ops
  that lower to `Put { key, before, after }` over 11 key kinds;
  transactions with locals, typed errors, named cascades, host-owned
  attributes and capabilities (§6).
- **Effects and history:** a pure `classify`; versions that name
  immutable states; checked replay; groups for OCR and plugin calls;
  value-validated commits with two stale policies; a byte budget that
  prunes (§7, §8).
- **Saves:** saves as outputs with a `SaveRecord`; a planner with
  signature impact and "Append anyway"; in-place appends with a journal;
  a removal check that fails closed; encryption kept; recovery v2 (§9,
  §11).
- **Rendering:** a realizer that renders edited pages from in-memory
  append sections, one engine pool, render keys, view inputs and a text
  service (§10).
- **Exports** as transforms on an export transaction (§12), and the
  content lexer, writer and fonts pulled forward from M11 (§13).
- **Consumers:** one commit door and one refresh in the app, one open, a
  `Session` for headless use, `sheaf edit` and `sheaf inspect`, and
  plugin API 0.4 as the only world (§14).
- **Moved out:** see Not in M10.5. Nothing is built for the agent, but
  nothing in the API needs reshaping for it (§14.4).
- **Size:** 20 slices, about 68 points (S = 1, M = 2, L = 3, XL = 5).

## Decided while planning (Greg, 2026-10-15)

1. **M10.5 designs and builds a clean edit API** for M11 onward:
   redaction, sanitize, encryption, signatures, in-place content
   editing, optimize, PDF/A, accessibility and batch. It's built from
   this plan and sliced like M9 and M10: one commit per slice, each with
   its checks.
2. **M10 pauses after slice 5 and resumes on the new API.** M10's PR
   merges to `main`, M10.5 is built from `main`, and M10 then resumes
   with slices 6+ on the new API (M10 slices 6+ on the new API, below;
   when exactly is proposal 11).
3. **Clean and correct beats compatibility,** since sheaf is pre-alpha.
   - No shims. The `Edit` enum, the WIT edit variant (sheaf stops
     serving 0.2 and 0.3), the plugin-api `Batch` and sheaf's private
     file data may all be redesigned.
   - No migration code for sheaf's own older formats unless it's nearly
     free.
   - Fixed: PDF interoperability (Acrobat, PDFium, pdf.js, and Acrobat
     round trips), the snapshot undo guarantees, and speed at about
     7,000 pages.
4. **Standard PDF keys first.** Private data only where PDF has no place
   for it.
5. **The generated-file cache is retired for derived sources.** Page
   content becomes part of the model.
6. **Plugins and the agent get semantic edits only, at first.** The API
   must be able to expand later.
7. **Existing features are re-expressed on the new API, and the old
   paths deleted,** within the milestone.

## The design

### 1. Starting point: the code at `a0955fc`

The facts the design relies on, checked at `a0955fc`:

- **Branch state.** HEAD is `a0955fc` on `m10`: M10 slices 3, 4 and 5
  (`32723da`, `70257fc`, `e5468e8`), then the pause commit.
- **Vendored lopdf:**
  - `encrypt_object` (encryption.rs:828), `decrypt_object` (:833), and
    `transform_object` (:734), which exempts only xref streams and
    `/Metadata`;
  - no `/EFF` handling;
  - `Object::Real(f32)` (object.rs:83);
  - the `object_streams` cache (reader.rs:364) and `Reader::get_object`
    (:1095);
  - the reader slices the buffer at the first `%PDF-` and treats xref
    offsets as relative to it (reader.rs:686).
- **`kept.rs`** (1,144 lines, plus 657 lines of tests in
  `kept/tests.rs`):
  `keep_only(pdf: &mut Pdf, kept)` (:21) needs a mutable parsed graph.
  It keeps `/Names /Templates` pages, cuts references to removed pages'
  annotations, and prunes structure elements in place.
- **`save.rs`** (5,061 lines; tests from :1736):
  - `build_rewrite` (:379) gives a primary page object to the first page
    in document order that shows it (`used.insert(src_page)`, :613).
    That is a live ownership bug (Found while planning, bug 3).
  - Every rewrite ends with `rules::take_out(&mut pdf, form_changed)`
    (:707).
  - `subset` (:86) sets `form_changed` when it leaves out pages with
    widgets.
  - `export_clean_copy` (:34) and `export_flattened` (:182) refuse the
    open file. `extract_pages` (:72) and split don't.
  - `Signed::{Ask, Rewrite, Append}` (:869). `Append` means "append even
    what a full save takes out (a removed page, a changed form's XFA
    copy)".
  - `WouldBreakSignatures` (:884). `save_to_path_noting` (:899) tries an
    incremental save, then catches the failure. `write_atomic` (:954)
    has no fsync.
- **`incremental.rs`** (683 lines):
  - It imports
    `LoadedSource, copy_page_dict, geom_of, is_identity, link_connectors, strip_sheaf_annots, write_outline, write_page_annots`
    from save (:15–18).
  - It calls `file::page_geom`,
    `forms::merge::{merge, copy_annots, annots_on}`,
    `save::{reserve_page_objects, copy_of, origins, needed, copy_derived_page}`
    and `forms::rules::{has, take_out}`.
  - `Mode::{Save, Append, Sidecar}` (:47–59). Append and Sidecar take
    out XFA and usage rights inside the section (:427–436).
  - The section writer is at :478–550 and records offsets as absolute
    positions in the buffer. The object writer is
    `write_object`/`write_dict`/`write_name` at :591–683.
- **`model.rs`** (4,462 lines):
  - `SourceId(pub u32)` (:19) and `SourceInfo` (:32).
  - Today's `DocState` (:151–176): pages, outline, per-page sheaf
    `annots`, `text_layers`, labels, `header_footer`,
    `removed_comments`, terminal `fields` and per-page `widgets`.
  - `Ids { next_page, next_outline, next_field, seed }` (:536–541).
  - `Snapshot`, with seven dirty flags and the sources map (:606–630),
    computed in `snapshot()` (:1112–1125).
  - `add_source` (:785); `script_sources`, `scripts_mixed` and
    `form_notice` (:810–900); `rebase_to_saved` (:1208–1263).
  - `apply_in_job` (:1434–1463) amends the cursor version **in place**
    and recomputes `marginal_bytes`. Its only amend guard is
    `saved != cursor`.
  - `HistoryStats.undone_source_bytes` is hard-coded to 0 (:1715).
    `marginal` uses 256-byte guesses (:1798–1814).
  - `take_in_fields` (:1841–1910) joins a source's existing copy-0 form
    when its pages arrive in runs. `name_apart` (:2002) takes the stem
    from the source path.
  - Every insert registers a fresh `SourceId` (`add_source` at
    docview.rs:2705, page_insert.rs and combine.rs:169), so that join
    applies only within one registration's runs, and inserting identical
    bytes twice gives two forms
    (`corpus_a_form_combined_with_itself_is_two_forms`).
  - The `DocState` struct literals are at :735 and :2437. `signed_from`
    is at :2813 and `next_copy` at :2821.
- **Forms.**
  - `forms::Widget` (forms/field.rs:259) carries
    `object, slot, rect, flags, da, mk, border, state, states, has_appearance, actions`.
  - `Field.copy` and `Field.under` are at :208 and :212. `has_scripts`
    reads widget actions.
  - `forms/rules.rs`: `drop_xfa` copies tooltips onto the output's own
    fields, and `drop_rights` decides `/SigFlags` from the output's own
    signed fields, only when `/UR3` or `/UR` was present.
- **sheaf-ops `combine`.**
  - The first file becomes the primary through `Document::new` and
    `set_initial_fields` (combine.rs:147–152).
  - `insert_runs` inserts a file's non-contiguous selection as
    contiguous runs that join one form (:208–245).
  - Each later file gets a fresh `add_source`. The app writes the result
    and opens it in a new window (combine_dialog.rs:296–300).
- **App and plugins.**
  - `jobs::run_job` runs one job at a time (jobs.rs:3, :45). OCR is its
    own run (ocr_run.rs).
  - `Latest<T>` is latest-wins (plugins.rs:1415). `sign_marks` is at
    :1039 and `keep_unseen` at :1066.
  - The hide replays are at docview.rs:298, :1722 and :1901. The
    header/footer overlay is at :5196 and `annot_elements` at :5329.
  - The layers panel writes a decrypted temp copy (layers_panel.rs:84).
    The attachments panel opens attachments from a `temp_dir` folder
    (attachments_panel.rs:449), the one user-requested temp file.
- **sheaf-instance.** `PROTOCOL = 1`, a 2 s timeout and a 1 MiB request
  (lib.rs:31–40). `socket_path` falls back to `temp_dir` (:356).
  `recent.rs` legitimately compares paths with `temp_dir`.
- **Lint config.** There is no `clippy.toml`.
- **WIT 0.3.** `annot-delete(annot-ref)` reaches only sheaf marks, and
  `annotations()` returns only sheaf marks, with `author`.
- **Private names written today:**
  - `/SheafAnnot`;
  - `/SheafStamp`, both as an artifact property and as a stamp XObject
    name (annot.rs:1161–1168, read back at file.rs:509);
  - `/SheafStampText` (the stamp library, stamp_lib.rs:22);
  - `/SheafOcr`, the OCR layer font's resource name (text_layer.rs:396);
  - `/SheafOcrLayer`, the marker on the OCR layer's streams (:398);
  - `/SheafSettings`, which holds a local image path inside an inline
    property list (watermark.rs:216);
  - `/SheafHeaderFooter`, `/SheafHF` and `/SheafPart`;
  - `/SheafTick<id>` as a stamp `/Name` (annot.rs:2276);
  - the resource names `/SheafFlat1`, `/SheafWMf`, `/SheafWMgs` and
    `/SheafWMim`.
- **The OCR layer's shape.** Today's OCR layer is
  `[q, original…, layer starting "Q"]` (text_layer.rs:584–603, :526).
  Both added streams carry `/SheafOcrLayer true`; the `q` stream is
  shared per file (:494), and `strip` removes both (:625–651).
  `/SheafOcr` is the glyphless font's resource name (:396), used by `Tf`
  inside the layer.
- **Recovery v1** writes `<name>.pdf.sheaf-recovery` next to the file
  (recovery.rs:2, :17) and checks only the base's length (:58–70).
- **Measured.** A full rewrite of the 3.5 MB synthetic 7k file takes
  0.49 s and 69 MB (1.54 s and 131 MB at 14k). The synthetic file has
  one font, one shared Resources dict, a classic xref and no
  annotations.

### 2. The shape

```
 callers: gestures · dialogs · jobs (OCR, trim, import) · `sheaf edit` JSON · plugins (WIT 0.4, JSON Op)
            │  Op (public, serde + schemars, sheaf-edit crate)          Transform (export-only: ExportTx)
            ▼
 resolve    BlobSel → BlobRef (Store, CLI `--with`, plugin asset grants); caps checked per resolved target
            ▼
 Tx         lower(op) over (DocState, immutable Sources, Store, IdAlloc, HostAttrs) — pure
            │  validate → Puts → named cascade rules to a fixed point → describe line
            │  records lowering reads, write before-values, and the caller's decision reads (by value)
            ▼
 Prim::Put { key, before, after }      ← the one primitive. History stores it; replay applies it and asserts `before`.
            ▼
 DocState   order · pages(PageDef) · geoms · page_annots · annots · fields · form · bookmarks · outline_roots
            · labels · generators            (im + Arc: O(1) clone, Send + Sync; records hold BlobRef/Src handles)
     │                                   │                                        │
 classify(key, before, after)      realize(state, page group, ViewInputs)    materialize(state, Profile, SaveTarget)
 = Effects (pure)                  = emit(group) as an in-memory append      = plan → emit → (Rewrite: keep_only)
     │                               section over the source bytes              → serialize → verify
 ChangeSet · save plan ·                 │                                        │
 approvals · emit_deps             EnginePool (PDFium, one lock)            bytes + SaveRecord (never a rebase)
```

**Twelve rules:**

1. **DocState is the document's definition.** One emitter derives every
   PDF object from it, on screen and on disk. DocState holds no object
   number that can go stale. Provenance (`ObjRef`) points only into
   immutable sources.
2. **Sources are immutable, lazily read and content-addressed.**
   - A file fact is a memoized pure function of a source's bytes, so
     facts can't "arrive late".
   - Records hold liveness handles (`Src`, `BlobRef`), so a snapshot or
     a step pins every byte it names.
3. **Two layers and one primitive.** About 25 public `Op`s lower to
   `Put { key, before, after }` over 11 key kinds. Ordered collections
   are values of keys, not splice primitives.
4. **History stores resolved prims with before-values, and a `VersionId`
   names one immutable state.**
   - Replay asserts before-values and never lowers.
   - A commit onto a cursor that moved since the transaction's base
     validates, **by value**, everything it read or overwrites.
5. **Effects are a pure function of `(key, before, after)`.** Dirty
   state, removal detection, signature impact, ChangeSets, render
   invalidation and the save plan are all folds of it.
6. **One frame below the page: PageSpace,** the unrotated default user
   space. Box and rotate edits never rewrite a stored coordinate.
   Rotation is absolute. Pointers, the clipboard and plugin helpers
   convert at the edge.
7. **No id is an ObjectId, a slot or an index.**
   - Ids written to files are random below 2^53 and written as PDF
     strings.
   - Session ids are counters, and no id is ever reused.
8. **A save is an output, not an edit.**
   - The model never re-expresses a version.
   - A `SaveRecord` drives the next save, and the version being saved is
     sealed until the save finishes.
9. **One emitter** feeds four outputs: full rewrite, incremental append,
   recovery section and render overlay.
10. **The screen equals the file.** The one exception: GPUI keeps
    drawing sheaf marks until M10 slice 12.
11. **Nothing touches disk** except the user's files (and their
    same-directory temp files for atomic writes), the recovery sidecar
    (encrypted when the file is) and the append journal. The other
    exceptions hold no document content, apart from an attachment the
    user opens (§10.6).
12. **Fail, never repair, and fail closed on removal.**
    - Unknown references and unreadable content are typed errors.
    - An object-level check vetoes any Append that would leave saved
      text readable.
    - The one time-boxed exception is the `'?'` glyph for unencodable
      text, until M10 slice 7 (§13).

**The registry rule.** Every cross-cutting function starts with an
exhaustive destructure of `DocState` (no `..`) or an exhaustive match on
`Key`. Those functions are `classify`, every cascade trigger,
`emit_roots`, `emit_deps`, `ChangeSet::from_keys`, `HeapSize`,
`check_invariants` and `render_key`. Adding a key kind then fails to
compile until each of them handles it.

**Nothing ships unproduced.** No key, enum variant, field or public type
ships without an M10.5 producer and consumer. §19 lists each public type
with what it replaces and what produces it. Later milestones add
variants additively; `#[non_exhaustive]` plus the registry rule make
each addition compile-checked.

### 3. The content model

#### 3.1 The Store, blobs and sources

```rust
// sheaf-edit/src/ids.rs, re-exported by sheaf-doc (slice 3 starts it in sheaf-doc/src/store.rs; slice 11 moves it, §6.1)
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord, Debug)]
pub struct BlobId { pub hash: u128 /* xxh3-128 */, pub len: u64 }      // serde: "b<32 hex>-<len>" (JSON-safe)
pub type SourceId = BlobId;                                             // a source IS a PDF blob

// sheaf-doc/src/store.rs
pub struct Store { blobs: RwLock<HashMap<BlobId, Weak<BlobInner>>> }   // per Document; outside undo
#[derive(Clone)] pub struct Blob(Arc<BlobInner>);
struct BlobInner { id: BlobId, bytes: Arc<[u8]>, kind: BlobKind, pdf: Mutex<Weak<Source>> }
pub enum BlobKind { Pdf { password: Option<Zeroizing<String>>, origin: Origin }, Image(ImageFormat),
                    Attachment { mime: Option<Arc<str>> } }
pub enum Origin { File(Arc<Path>), Memory(MemoryKind) }     // where it was FIRST interned: a hint, never a display name
pub enum MemoryKind { Blank, Images, Scan, Recovered, Library, Summary, Derived /* transitional, gone in slice 17 */ }

/// What records hold: pins the blob; Eq/Ord/Hash by id; serializes as its BlobId.
#[derive(Clone)] pub struct BlobRef(Blob);
/// A parsed PDF source: pins its Blob; Eq/Ord/Hash by id; serializes as its SourceId.
#[derive(Clone)] pub struct Src(Arc<Source>);

impl Store {
    /// On an id hit the bytes are COMPARED; a mismatch is Err(BlobCollision).
    pub fn intern(&self, bytes: Arc<[u8]>, kind: BlobKind) -> Result<Blob, BlobCollision>;
    pub fn get(&self, id: BlobId) -> Option<Blob>;           // None once nothing holds it
    pub fn source(&self, b: &Blob) -> Result<Src, OpenError>; // parses lazily; re-parses only if every Src died
}
```

A **blob** is a byte string the document uses (a PDF, an image, an
attached file), named by its content hash. A **source** is a PDF blob,
parsed.

**Liveness is the Arc.**
- A blob lives while a `Blob`, `BlobRef` or `Src` exists anywhere. That
  covers a kept snapshot, a step's prims, an open `Tx` (which pins the
  blobs its caller interned with `Tx::pin`), an engine, and the app's
  clipboard blob cache (§14.1).
- The `Store` keeps only `Weak` references, so there is no sweep to
  forget.
- `Source` holds its `Blob`, and `BlobInner` holds only a
  `Weak<Source>`, so there is no cycle.
- `HeapSize` counts each blob once, by id.

**Interning is outside undo** ("deleting a shape never deletes its
asset", tldraw's rule). Callers intern off-thread before lowering. Op
JSON names blobs with `BlobSel` (§6.6), which a resolve step turns into
a `BlobRef` before lowering. Lowering never sees bytes.

**One source per distinct file.** Inserting the same bytes twice gives
one `Source`. Identity on the page side comes from `PageDef.copy`, and
on the form side from `FormInst` (§3.7), never from the source. Display
names (rename stems, notices) come from the op's
`PageSource::File { name }`, the name the user picked, never from the
blob's first `Origin`. They are kept per form instance in
`FormDoc.names` (§3.7).

```rust
// sheaf-doc/src/source/
pub struct Source { blob: Blob, crypt: Option<Arc<Crypt>>, pdf: LazyPdf, facts: Facts }
impl Source {
    pub fn id(&self) -> SourceId;
    pub fn base_sound(&self) -> bool;                                 // false when lopdf rebuilt the xref
    pub fn xref(&self) -> &XrefFacts;                                 // kind, start, size, /ID, header offset, hybrid, linearized
    pub fn crypt(&self) -> Option<&Arc<Crypt>>;                       // StmF, StrF, EFF, key, /ID: kept, never stripped
    pub fn object(&self, id: ObjectId) -> Result<Arc<Object>>;
    pub fn raw_span(&self, id: ObjectId) -> Option<Range<usize>>;    // absolute in the buffer; None for ObjStm members
    pub fn page_count(&self) -> u32;
    pub fn page(&self, i: u32) -> Result<Arc<PageFacts>>;            // ObjectId, parent chain, inherited attrs, Geom, /Contents,
                                                                      // /Annots, /StructParents, repeat index
    pub fn annots(&self, i: u32) -> Result<Arc<[BaseAnnot]>>;        // mark (v2/v1/stale), foreign, link, widget
    pub fn blocks(&self, i: u32) -> Result<Arc<PageBlocks>>;         // lexical classification (§13); never at open
    pub fn form(&self) -> Result<Arc<SourceForm>>;                   // forms::read + node ids + /DR handles + /CO + XFA facts
    pub fn outline(&self) -> Result<Arc<[BaseBookmark]>>;
    pub fn labels(&self) -> Result<Option<Arc<[BaseLabel]>>>;
    pub fn generators(&self) -> Result<Arc<[Generator]>>;            // /SheafGenerators + /SheafBlock stream markers
    pub fn signatures(&self) -> Result<Arc<[SigFact]>>;              // field, byte range, DocMDP P, FieldMDP, UR3 TransformParams
    pub fn revisions(&self) -> Result<Arc<[RevisionInfo]>>;          // the /Prev chain (forms' EarlierValues)
    pub fn conformance(&self) -> Result<Conformance>;                // XMP pdfaid / pdfuaid claims
}
```

**`LazyPdf`** is a patch to vendored lopdf.
- It keeps the parsed xref and trailer, and serves `get(id)` through
  `Reader::get_object`, with cycle detection, per-object decryption
  (strings in stream dicts included, which closes lopdf #603) and the
  `object_streams` cache.
- It honors `/EFF` for `/Type /EmbeddedFile` streams.
- It records the header offset, and `raw_span` and xref offsets are
  translated by it.
- It runs over a byte source: an in-memory `Arc<[u8]>`, or a
  positional-read file handle. The positional handle is used only to
  read a saved file's old objects for the removal check (§11.9).
- One `Limits` value covers every reader: lazy objects, the page tree,
  forms, the content lexer and JSON.
- When lopdf has to rebuild the xref, the file falls back to an eager
  load with `base_sound == false`. Such a file is never appended to.
- `Object::Real` becomes `f64` (about 10 vendor sites and 46 sheaf
  sites).

**Facts are never history.** Whichever thread asks first computes a
fact. Expensive facts, such as classifying the content of 7,000 pages,
or an inserted file's annotations and form, are forced off the UI
thread: in the resolve phase of a job, in a background dialog preview,
or in the save planner.

**Open never lexes content.** `generators()` reads `/SheafGenerators`
and the `/SheafBlock` keys in content-stream *dicts* only, with no
decompression. Blocks that another tool merged into other streams are
found later, by the `blocks` fact.

#### 3.2 DocState

```rust
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct DocState {
    order: im::Vector<PageId>,                                  // Key::Order
    pages: im::HashMap<PageId, Arc<PageDef>>,                   // Key::Page(id)
    geoms: im::HashMap<PageId, Arc<Geom>>,                      // Key::Geom(id)
    page_annots: im::HashMap<PageId, im::Vector<AnnotId>>,      // Key::PageAnnots(id): /Annots order
    annots: im::HashMap<AnnotId, AnnotRec>,                     // Key::Annot(id): marks, foreign, links, widgets
    fields: im::OrdMap<FieldId, Arc<FieldNode>>,                // Key::Field(id): every node of the tree
    form: Arc<FormDoc>,                                         // Key::Form
    bookmarks: im::HashMap<OutlineId, Arc<Bookmark>>,           // Key::Bookmark(id)
    outline_roots: im::Vector<OutlineId>,                       // Key::OutlineRoots
    labels: Option<Arc<[LabelSpec]>>,                           // Key::Labels
    generators: im::OrdMap<GenId, Arc<Generator>>,              // Key::Generator(id)
    idx: Indexes,   // derived, not part of Eq: position map per `order` Arc; /NM → AnnotId; full name → FieldId
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord, Debug)]
pub enum Key { Order, Page(PageId), Geom(PageId), PageAnnots(PageId), Annot(AnnotId), Field(FieldId),
               Form, Bookmark(OutlineId), OutlineRoots, Labels, Generator(GenId) }
```

**Why these keys.** A key is the unit of value validation, dirtiness and
invalidation.
- `Geom` is separate from `Page`, so a crop or rotate during an OCR run
  doesn't touch what the OCR flush validated.
- `PageAnnots` is separate from `Page`.
- Bookmarks and field nodes are keyed per item.
- `Labels` and `Form` are single records, edited as wholes.
- `Order` and `OutlineRoots` are single values. Work that only needs
  "insert somewhere" commits with `Relower` (§8.5) instead of
  conflicting on them.

**Construction and access.**
- DocState is built only through `DocState::builder()` (open,
  `ExportTx`) and `apply(&Prim)`.
- Reads go through accessors: `page_count`, `page_at`, `pos_of(id)`
  (O(1) after one O(n) build per `order` Arc), `def`, `geom`,
  `annots_on`, `annot`, `annot_by_nm(page, nm)`, `field`, `field_named`,
  `bookmarks_preorder` and `generators_on(page)`.
- About 160 sites read today's fields directly (`state.pages` 96,
  `outline` 25, `text_layers` 10, and about 30 others). They move to the
  accessors.

#### 3.3 Pages: `PageDef` and `Geom`

```rust
pub struct PageDef {
    pub base: PageBase,                   // blank/image pages are memory sources (§3.9)
    pub copy: u32,                        // 0 = the base page object itself; n ≥ 1 = the nth private copy
    pub content: Arc<ContentStack>,       // ContentStack::EMPTY (shared Arc) when untouched
    digest: OnceLock<u128>,
}
pub struct PageBase { pub src: Src, pub index: u32 /* 0-based, in src */ }
pub struct Geom {                         // PageSpace; crop clipped to media; a crop outside media is ignored
    pub media: Rect, pub crop: Rect, pub trim: Option<Rect>, pub bleed: Option<Rect>, pub art: Option<Rect>,
    pub rotate: Rotation,                 // ABSOLUTE, as it will be written
    pub user_unit: f32,
}
```

**An untouched page costs almost nothing.**
- Its `PageDef`, `Geom` and annotation list are `ptr_eq` to the root's,
  and its content is the shared `EMPTY`.
- The emitter writes nothing for it.
- The realizer renders it from the base engine.
- It takes about 200 bytes per page, about 1.4 MB at 7,000 pages, shared
  by every version.

**The copy index is fixed at creation.** Copy 0 always owns the base
page object. If copy 0 is deleted, copy 1 is still written as a new
object of its own. Moving pages never changes `/P`, links or the object
map. A page tree that lists one page object twice (invalid, but real)
opens as copy 0 and copy 1, with a notice.

**Other page-dict keys.** `/Tabs`, and other keys sheaf may set later,
arrive with their first producer (M10 slice 10). They come as
`KeyPatch(OrdMap<Name, Option<PatchValue>>)`, whose values are symbolic
(`PatchValue` references are `ObjRef | PageId | BlobRef | FormRef`,
never raw object numbers), plus a classify role row per patched key
(§20). No patch type ships in M10.5, because nothing would produce it.

#### 3.4 The content stack

A **content stack** is a page's content as a definition: filters applied
to the base page's `/Contents`, and layers drawn under and over it.

```rust
pub struct ContentStack {
    pub filters: im::Vector<Filter>,     // applied to the base /Contents, in order
    pub under: im::Vector<Layer>,        // drawn before the base, first = bottom
    pub over: im::Vector<Layer>,         // drawn after the base, isolated from it by q/Q
}
pub struct Layer { pub id: BlockId, pub kind: BlockKind, pub body: Static }
pub enum BlockKind { Ocr, Flattened }
pub enum Static {
    OcrText(Arc<TextLayer>),             // invisible text (Tr 3, one glyphless Type0 per file); words in PageSpace
    Placements(Arc<[Placement]>),        // Form XObjects placed by BBox/Matrix/Rect (flatten)
}
pub struct Placement { pub form: FormRef, pub matrix: Affine, pub tag: PlaceTag /* Artifact | Untagged */ }
pub enum FormRef {
    Base(ObjRef),                        // an appearance stream already in a source
    Blob(BlobRef),                       // a one-page PDF blob: its page as a form (library stamps)
    Pinned(Arc<PinnedAp>),               // a derived appearance, its inputs captured when flattened
}
pub struct PinnedAp { pub inputs: ApInputs /* the Mark; or Widget + terminal value/display + /AS + FormDoc /DA /Q /DR
                                               handles */, pub digest: u128 }
pub enum Filter {
    DropMarked(MarkedSel),               // remove marked-content ranges of the base (lexical splice, §13)
    DropStreams(StreamSel),              // drop whole base /Contents entries
    StripLegacy(LegacySel),              // RemovePrivateData on legacy sheaf content: drop private keys from inline
                                         // property lists; rename private resource names used by Tf/Do operands
}
pub enum LegacySel { Marked(MarkedSel), Stream(StreamSel) }
pub enum MarkedSel {
    Pagination { kinds: EnumSet<PaginationKind>, origins: EnumSet<PagOrigin> },
    SheafGen(GenId), SheafOcr,           // sheaf blocks inside streams another tool merged
}
pub enum PaginationKind { Header, Footer, Watermark, Background }
pub enum PagOrigin { Sheaf, SheafLegacy, Acrobat, Authored }      // §13 classifies
pub enum StreamSel { SheafBlock(BlockId), SheafGen(GenId), SheafOcr /* + legacy /SheafOcrLayer and its 'q' */, Isolation }
```

**A stack holds definitions, not bytes.**
- The content writer (§13) derives layer bytes deterministically and
  caches them by input digest.
- Additions never re-encode the original content, so an Append is still
  possible.
- `Pinned` captures the inputs, not the bytes. The emitter re-derives
  the identical form from the layer alone, so a flattened appearance
  never depends on a deleted annotation or on a cache.
- `check_invariants` asserts that no `AnnotId` or `FieldId` is
  referenced from a ContentStack.

**Drawing order is fixed:**

```
generators(Under) → static under → QOpen^open → base′ → QClose^close → static over → generators(Over)
open  = 1 + d          close = 1 + d + net          d = max(0, −min_prefix)
```

`Balance { net, min_prefix }` is base′'s lexical q/Q balance (§13): the
net count, and the lowest the running count goes. Some worked bases:

| Base | net | min_prefix | open | close |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q … cm` (no Q) | +1 | 0 | 1 | 2 |
| `Q q` | 0 | −1 | 2 | 2 |
| `Q` | −1 | −1 | 2 | 1 |

A base that can't be lexed fails the op that needs isolation. There is
no "unknown" count.

Isolation streams are written once per materialization, and each is
marked (`/SheafBlock << /K /Isolate >>`, §11.4). Before reassembling,
the emitter sets aside sheaf's own isolation streams and blocks from the
base, so nesting never grows across save, reopen and edit cycles.

**Growth.** M11 adds `ContentStack.rewrite`, "the new base", which
absorbs the filters present when it is set; later filters apply to its
output. It also adds `Static::Ops`, a definition evaluated per page at
emit (§20). Neither exists in M10.5.

#### 3.5 Generators: parametric, document-level content

A **generator** is content defined once for the document and laid out
per page at emit time: the header/footer (with Bates) and the watermark
or background.

```rust
pub struct Generator {
    pub id: GenId,                                   // random < 2^53; written as a PDF string
    pub spec: GenSpec,                               // HeaderFooter(Arc<HeaderFooterSpec>) | Watermark(Arc<WatermarkSpec>)
    pub z: Z,                                        // Under | Over  (background = watermark Under)
    pub scope: Scope,                                // All (includes pages added later) | Pages(im::OrdSet<PageId>)
    pub frozen: Frozen,                              // decided at apply: date and file tokens; Bates numbers
    pub replaces: EnumSet<PaginationKind>,           // Acrobat-ADDED artifacts of these kinds on covered pages are
                                                     // dropped at emit (never authored ones)
}
pub struct Frozen { pub date: Arc<str>, pub file: Arc<str>, pub bates: Option<Arc<im::OrdMap<PageId, u64>>> }
pub struct HeaderFooterSpec { pub texts: [Arc<str>; 6], pub font: FontChoice, pub size: f32, pub color: Color,
                              pub margins: Margins, pub first_number: u32, pub bates: Option<BatesSpec> }
pub struct BatesSpec { pub prefix: Arc<str>, pub suffix: Arc<str>, pub digits: u8, pub start: u64 }
pub struct WatermarkSpec { pub content: WatermarkContent, pub position: GridPos /* 3×3 grid, displayed frame */ }
pub enum WatermarkContent {
    Text { text: Arc<str>, font: FontChoice, size: f32, color: Color, opacity: f32, angle: f32 },
    Image { image: ImageRef, scale: f32, opacity: f32 },
    Page { page: PageForm, scale: f32, opacity: f32 },
}
pub enum ImageRef { Blob(BlobRef), Placed(ObjRef) }        // Placed: the XObject a saved block draws (rebound at open)
pub enum PageForm { Blob { pdf: BlobRef, index: u32 }, Placed(ObjRef) }
impl Generator {
    pub fn needs(&self) -> CtxNeeds;                          // which PageCtx fields its tokens read
    pub fn layout(&self, page: &PageCtx) -> Drawn;            // pure, deterministic; may be empty
    pub fn recipe_digest(&self, page: PageId) -> u128;        // spec + z + this page's frozen values
}
pub struct PageCtx { pub geom: Geom, pub position: u32, pub count: u32, pub label: Arc<str>, pub bates: Option<u64> }
pub struct Drawn { pub ops: Arc<[u8]>, pub res: Arc<[(Name, Res)]>, pub layout_digest: u128 }
```

**Live and frozen tokens** (proposal 8).
- Page-number, count and label tokens (`<<1>>`, `<<N>>`, `<<label>>`)
  are **live**: they are evaluated against the document being written,
  whether that is the binder, an extract or a print branch.
- Bates numbers, `<<date>>` and `<<file>>` are **frozen at apply**.
  Reapplying the header is how to renumber.
- `needs()` says which `PageCtx` fields a spec's tokens read. A
  watermark without tokens needs only geometry, so a reorder neither
  re-renders nor re-emits it.

**Orientation.** Layout happens in the page's displayed frame (the
visible box and absolute `/Rotate`) at emit time. Headers and watermarks
stay upright after a rotate, and a dialog's preview equals the result.

**Recognition after a save or reopen.**
- Each drawn block is its own stream. Its dict carries
  `/SheafBlock << /K /Gen /G (g<hex>) /L <layout hex> /R <recipe hex> /N (000123) >>`
  (§11.4 has the dict for every kind of block). `/G`, `/L`, `/R` and
  `/N` are strings, so no integer goes past ISO 32000-1 Annex C's ±2³¹
  and a PDFium or Acrobat re-save can't round them. `/N` is present only
  with Bates.
- Identical layouts share one block stream: a block is keyed by its
  layout digest.
- The content is wrapped as `/Artifact /ShP<n> BDC … EMC`. `/ShP<n>` is
  a **named** property list in the page's `/Resources /Properties`:
  `<< /Type /Pagination /Subtype /Header|/Footer|/Watermark|/Background /SheafGen (g<hex>) >>`.
  Acrobat, pdf.js and sheaf's classifier recognize it even after another
  tool merges `/Contents`, and `RemovePrivateData` strips `/SheafGen`
  with a dict edit, never a content rewrite.
- A covered page with nothing to draw gets an empty marked block, so a
  `Pages` scope rebuilds exactly.
- On open, `Source::generators()` reads the specs from
  `/SheafGenerators` (scope `all` or `pages`). It rebuilds `Pages`
  scopes and the frozen Bates map from each page's block (`/N`), keyed
  by that session's PageIds.
- It rebinds `ImageRef::Placed` and `PageForm::Placed` to the XObject in
  the recognized block's resources, never to object numbers in the JSON.
- If another tool merged a block's stream, its Bates number can't be
  recovered. The plan phase then reports it, and Update re-lays that
  page from the spec, with a notice.

**The emitter compares before writing.**
- If a page's layout digest equals the base block's `/L`, the base
  stream is referenced and nothing is written.
- If it differs, the old block stream is set aside and the new one
  drawn: a reorder that changed "Page n of N", a crop that moved the
  edge, or a rotate that turned the frame.
- If the recipe digest `/R` is unchanged, that difference is a live
  redraw (`LIVE_REDRAW`, §7.1).

**Unbacked blocks are kept.** A sheaf block in the base that no
generator in the model backs is kept as foreign content.
`RemoveHeaderFooter` and `RemoveWatermark` remove it through explicit
filters.

**Unencodable text** draws `'?'` with a one-time notice, for marks and
generators alike, until M10 slice 7's fallback font (§13). Emit is
total: a later label change that makes a live token unencodable can't
make the document unsaveable.

**Scope.** `Scope::All` covers pages inserted later. `Pages(OrdSet)`
gives an O(log n) `covers`.

#### 3.6 Annotations: one collection, one id space

```rust
pub struct AnnotRec { pub page: PageId, pub body: AnnotBody }
pub enum AnnotBody { Mark(Arc<Mark>), Foreign(Arc<Foreign>), Widget(Arc<Widget>) }
pub struct Mark { pub kind: AnnotKind, pub style: Style, pub contents: Arc<str>, pub author: Arc<str>,
                  pub created: PdfDate, pub modified: PdfDate,
                  pub nm: Arc<str> /* "sheaf:<id>" for marks sheaf made; the source's /NM for adopted and imported ones */,
                  pub reply_to: Option<AnnotId>, pub partner: Option<AnnotId>, pub turn: Rotation,
                  pub file: Option<BlobRef> /* attachment */, pub stamp: Option<FormRef> /* page stamp */,
                  pub src: Option<ObjRef> /* identity across saves; source keys kept (below) */ }
pub struct Foreign { pub src: ObjRef, pub copy: u32, pub subtype: Name, pub rect: Rect, pub flags: AnnotFlags,
                     pub nm: Option<Arc<str>>, pub reply_to: Option<AnnotId>, pub summary: Arc<ForeignSummary> }
pub struct Widget { pub field: FieldId, pub src: Option<ObjRef>, pub inst: FormInst,
                    pub merged: bool,      // its dict IS its terminal field's dict (one object)
                    pub rect: Rect, pub flags: AnnotFlags, pub mk: Arc<Mk>, pub da: Option<Arc<str>>,
                    pub state: Option<Name> /* /AS */, pub states: Arc<[Name]>, pub props: Arc<WidgetProps> }
pub struct WidgetProps { pub border: Option<Border>, pub actions: Arc<[(Trigger, Action)]>, pub has_appearance: bool }
```

A **mark** is an annotation sheaf made and owns. A **foreign**
annotation is one another tool made.

**Other tools' annotations are first-class records,** read into the root
at open.
- Deleting one is `Put Annot(id) → None`, and its replies cascade by id.
- Links are `Foreign` records with subtype Link. The emitter remaps or
  drops them (§11.3). A modeled `Link` body arrives with M13's first
  link-editing op.

**Widgets are annotations.** There is one `AnnotId` space, and `/Annots`
order is one `Vector<AnnotId>`. PDFium slots and the tab-order fallback
are derived from it. `WidgetProps` carries `/BS` or `/Border`, `/A` and
`/AA`, and whether an appearance exists. `Field::has_scripts`, M10 slice
6's borders and the action-driven slices read it.

**A mark with `src` keeps the source's other keys.**
- The emitter starts from the source dict and overwrites the keys sheaf
  owns for that kind:
  - `/Rect`, `/Contents`, `/C`, `/CA` and the geometry keys;
  - `/M`, `/T`, `/NM`, `/IRT` and `/RT`;
  - `/AP` and `/SheafAnnot`.
- It keeps `/DS`, `/RD`, `/IT`, `/Measure`, `/Popup` and unknown keys.
- When `/Contents` differs from the source's, it drops `/RC` (rich
  text), so Acrobat can't keep showing the old text and the old text
  doesn't survive in the new emission.
- Adopting another tool's comment (`AdoptAnnotations`) gives a mark with
  `src` under the same rule. It keeps the comment's `/NM`, so on reopen
  it is recognized as a mark by its `/SheafAnnot` v2, whose JSON carries
  its id, as v1's does.

**`/SheafAnnot` v2** holds the mark's JSON in PageSpace, with `"v":2`
and a staleness digest `"std"`.
- **The digest is canonical.** It covers the standard keys sheaf wrote,
  normalized:
  - numbers rounded to 1/64 pt and colors to 1/1000;
  - text strings decoded to NFC Unicode, whatever their encoding or
    encryption;
  - `/Rect` with its corners normalized.

  So a mere re-serialization by Acrobat or Preview (`100` → `100.0`, f32
  rounding, a UTF-16BE `/Contents`) keeps a sheaf mark a sheaf mark.
- **A mismatch on read** means another tool edited the mark after sheaf
  wrote it. The mark opens as **Foreign**: visible, deletable and
  adoptable. sheaf never reverts the other tool's edit.
- **v1 payloads** are converted on read (proposal 21). Geometry comes
  from the standard keys, which are already PageSpace; the JSON supplies
  only what PDF can't hold. Staleness compares the JSON's `modified`
  with `/M`.
- **Ticks** keep their identity in the v2 JSON (`"tick": id`). Their
  stamp `/Name` is `/Tick`, not `/SheafTick<id>`.
- **Duplicate `sheaf:<id>`.** `/NM` is unique only per page, and
  Acrobat's Insert Pages duplicates it. The first occurrence in document
  order keeps its id. Later ones get fresh `AnnotId`s and a new `/NM` on
  their next write, with one notice.

#### 3.7 Forms: three layers, explicit instances

1. **`SourceForm`** is an immutable fact per source: the `forms::read`
   output plus node ids, `/DR` font handles (`ObjRef`), `/CO`, XFA
   facts, earlier-revision values, signatures and template pages.
2. **`FieldNode` records** live in DocState. One `FieldId` space covers
   every node.
3. **Values** live inside those records.

```rust
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord, Debug)]
pub struct FormInst { pub src: SourceId, pub copy: u32 }   // copy 0 = the source's first instance (for the primary, its
                                                           // own objects in place); every other instance, and any
                                                           // instance of a non-primary source, is written as new objects
pub struct FieldNode {
    pub parent: Option<FieldId>, pub partial: Arc<str>,      // /T as it will be written; full names derived
    pub src: Option<ObjRef>, pub inst: FormInst,             // which instance of which source's form
    pub kids: im::Vector<FieldId>,
    pub term: Option<Arc<Terminal>>,
}
pub struct Terminal { pub kind: FieldKind, pub flags: FieldFlags, pub value: Value, pub default: Value,
                      pub display: Option<Arc<str>>, pub props: Arc<FieldProps>,
                      pub widgets: im::Vector<AnnotId>,
                      pub outside: u16 }   // widgets on page templates: not modeled, kept by keep_only, never cascaded
pub struct FormDoc { pub roots: im::Vector<FieldId>, pub co: im::Vector<FieldId>, pub da: Option<Arc<str>>,
                     pub q: Option<u8>, pub dr: Arc<[(Name, FontHandle)]>, pub need_appearances: bool,
                     pub sig_flags: u32, pub xfa: Option<XfaState>, pub scripts: ScriptState, pub damaged: bool,
                     pub names: im::OrdMap<FormInst, Arc<str>> }   // the file name each instance came from (the open's
                                                                   // for the primary, the op's `name` for inserts; a
                                                                   // duplicate inherits its original's): rename stems, notices
pub enum ScriptState { On, Off { instances: Arc<[FormInst]> } }   // off when scripts came from > 1 instance
```

A **form instance** (`FormInst`) is one copy of one source's form in the
document.

**A form instance is explicit.**
- `InsertPages`, `ReplacePages` and `DuplicatePages` create one new
  `FormInst` per op and source instance. Its `copy` is the next unused
  copy number for that source in the state.
- A file's non-contiguous page selection is one
  `InsertPages { pages: RangeSet }` (§6.1), so its widgets join one
  instance. Inserting the same bytes again is another op and another
  instance, renamed apart.
- `Join` (adding pages to an existing instance) has no M10.5 producer
  once page sets exist. It can be added later.

**Owned AcroForm keys** are `/Fields`, `/CO`, `/DA`, `/Q`, `/DR`,
`/NeedAppearances` and `/SigFlags`. They are always emitted from
`FormDoc` (§11.3). `/XFA` and `/Perms` are governed by save transforms
(§11.8).

**Page templates** (`/Names /Templates`) are pages outside the page
tree. They aren't modeled: `keep_only` keeps them, and their widgets
count in `Terminal.outside`. Spawning a page from a template arrives
with M10's script slices (`PageSource::Template`).

**M10 slices 4–5's mechanisms, re-expressed:**

| Today | Becomes |
|---|---|
| `Field.copy` | `FieldNode.inst` / `Widget.inst` |
| `Field.under` | a real parent node |
| `Document.forms`, `set_source_form` | lowering reads `Src::form()` |
| `take_in_fields`' join branch | one op per file selection (`pages: RangeSet`) |
| `name_apart`, `bring_fields`, `take_in_fields` | lowering helpers; renames recorded as prims; stem from `FormDoc.names` (the op's `name` for inserts) |
| `scripts_mixed`, `script_sources` | `FormDoc.scripts` |
| `form_notice`, `EditOutcome.forms_renamed/signatures_in` | `Notice::{FormRenamed, CopiesRenamed, SignatureNotValidHere, ScriptsOff}` (§6.5) |
| `signed_from`, `next_copy` | lowering helpers over `FormDoc` and `SourceForm` |

#### 3.8 Outline and labels

```rust
pub struct Bookmark { pub title: Arc<str>, pub target: Option<Target>, pub color: Option<Color>, pub style: TextStyle,
                      pub open: bool, pub kids: im::Vector<OutlineId>,
                      pub src: Option<ObjRef> /* original item: /A, /SE and unknown keys survive */ }
pub enum Target { Page { page: PageId, view: Option<DestView /* PageSpace */> }, Kept /* an /A or named dest as read */ }
pub struct LabelSpec { pub start: PageId, pub style: LabelStyle, pub prefix: Arc<str>, pub first: u32 }
```

Labels are read in full at open. An Append re-emits `/PageLabels`
whenever `Order` changes and labels exist (`emit_deps`, §7.2).

#### 3.9 Memory sources, cost and accounting

**Blank and image pages are memory sources.** Blank pages,
images-to-PDF, scans, placed library stamps and the comment summary are
small PDFs built in memory, interned as `Memory(kind)` blobs and
inserted with `InsertPages`. Nothing is written to a cache.

**Cost at 7,000 pages:**

| Operation | DocState cost | Append cost |
|---|---|---|
| Open | 7,000 PageDef + Geom (≈2 MB) | — (page tree walk, no content parsed) |
| Reorder all | one `Order` value | changed `/Pages` nodes, plus `/PageLabels` if labels exist, plus, for each live-token generator, every covered page whose layout changed (its block stream + page dict) |
| Rotate all | 7,000 `Geom` | 7,000 page dicts, plus generator blocks re-laid in the turned frame |
| Crop 10 pages | 10 `Geom` | 10 page dicts (+ their generator blocks if the visible edge moved) |
| Watermark all | one `Generator` | one block stream per distinct layout, one font/ExtGState/image per file, one Resources dict per distinct base Resources, 7,000 page dicts |
| OCR all | 7,000 PageDef with `Arc<TextLayer>` | one block per page, one glyphless font per file |
| Fill one of 20,000 fields (M10) | one `FieldNode` + widget | field dict + widget + AP (one object when merged) |

**Accounting (§8.7)** splits history memory into what trimming frees and
what only pruning frees:
- `snapshot_bytes` is the im nodes and indexes of a kept snapshot not
  shared with its neighbors.
- `step_bytes` is the deep bytes uniquely retained by a version's prims,
  Arc-deduplicated by pointer.
- `blob_bytes` counts each blob once.
- `HistoryStats` reports `snapshot_bytes`, `step_bytes`, `blob_bytes`,
  `undone_source_bytes` (reachable only from undone branches) and
  `engine_bytes`.

### 4. Identity

| Id | Allocation | In the file | Stable across |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PageId(u64)` | `IdAlloc` counter | no | the session, including saves |
| `AnnotId(u64)` | marks: random in [1, 2^53). Others' annotations, links and widgets read at open: an rng seeded by the source hash, so reopening an identical file gives identical ids. Every import, duplicate or insert gets fresh ids. Duplicate `sheaf:` names get fresh ids (§3.6). | marks sheaf made: `/NM (sheaf:<id>)`; adopted and imported marks: their own `/NM`, with the id in `/SheafAnnot` v2 | everything; marks across sessions |
| `FieldId(u64)` | counter; open numbers nodes in tree order | full name | session |
| `OutlineId(u64)` | counter | no | session |
| `GenId`, `BlockId` | random in [1, 2^53) | `/SheafGenerators`, `/SheafBlock /G` and `/B` (as strings, §11.4) | sessions |
| `SourceId = BlobId` | content hash, byte-compared | no | deterministic |
| `FormInst { src, copy }` | next copy for `src` at lowering | no | session |
| `VersionId` | counter; names one immutable state | no | session |
| `GroupId` | counter | no | session |
| `DocInstance(u64)` | random per open | no | part of every version token |

- **One `IdAlloc`, in an `Arc` with atomics.** Lowering allocates every
  id and stores it in a prim's after-value, so replay never allocates.
  Counters never rewind.
- **Pinned allocation for re-lowering.** A `Relower` commit (§8.5)
  replays the ids the first lowering allocated, in allocation order per
  kind, before drawing fresh ones.
- **Ids are never derived from object numbers.**
- **Uniqueness.** A random `AnnotId` is checked against `annots` in
  O(1). If two transactions mint the same id, the second commit fails
  its `before: None` check as a conflict.
- **A version token is `{ instance, version }`.** Since a VersionId
  names one immutable state, the token alone says whether anything
  changed. Snapshots, plugin reads, autosave skips and cache keys all
  use it.
- **`ObjKey`** is the materializer's identity for what it writes
  (§11.3). Ids and object numbers meet only in the `SaveRecord`'s map.
- **No sub-page content ids in M10.5.** Sub-page identity stops at
  blocks (`/SheafBlock`, `MarkedSel`). Descriptors arrive with M11's
  interpreter.

### 5. PageSpace: one frame below the page

**PageSpace** is the page's default user space: points, y up, before
`/Rotate`, independent of every box. It is the space of `/MediaBox`,
`/CropBox`, `/Rect`, `/QuadPoints` and `/XYZ`, and the space widgets
already use. Everything anchored to a page is stored in it:
- marks, others' annotation rects and widgets;
- OCR words (with `upright` for reading order);
- bookmark `DestView` and link rects;
- generator placement, which is computed;
- text-service glyph boxes, so the 16× device trick goes;
- region events and query results;
- Op JSON.

```rust
impl Geom {
    pub fn visible(&self) -> Rect;                 // crop ∩ media
    pub fn shown(&self) -> PageSize;               // visible, rotated
    pub fn to_view(&self) -> Affine;               // PageSpace → displayed (top-left of visible box, y down, after rotate)
    pub fn from_view(&self) -> Affine;
}
```

- **Displayed space is derived from `Geom::to_view` alone.**
  `save::geom_of`, `file::page_geom` and `annot::PageGeom` collapse into
  `Geom`.
- **Rotation is absolute model data.** The render `Job.rotation` is
  still a delta, `geom.rotate − base /Rotate`, so rotating a page needs
  no realization, and rotation is not in the render key.
- **A crop is a box edit.** Nothing stored moves. `Rebase.shift`,
  `TextLayer::shifted` and rebasing via `AnnotKind::translated` are
  deleted. (`translated` stays for drag and paste.)
- **The edges convert once, and there is more than one edge:**
  - **Pointer:** through `from_view`, composed with the pane's view
    rotation.
  - **Clipboard:** copied marks carry their geometry in the *source
    page's view frame*. Paste converts through the target page's
    `from_view`, which also derives `turn`, so a mark pasted onto a page
    with another `/Rotate` or CropBox origin keeps its visual position.
  - **Moves:** `MoveMarks { by: Delta }` with
    `Delta = Page(Vec2) | View(Vec2)`. A `View` delta is converted per
    mark through its own page's frame. Moving to another page maps
    through the source page's `to_view` and the target's `from_view`.
  - **Plugins:** WIT `page-info` carries `to-view` and `from-view`
    matrices. sheaf-plugin-api provides `to_view`/`from_view` helpers,
    and text and region results can return view rects on request.
    Placing something "at the top right as seen" therefore needs no
    `/Rotate` arithmetic.
  - **OCR:** pixels convert through the `Geom` and render transform
    captured when the page was rendered, never the commit-time `Geom`.
  - **XFDF and FDF:** rects are already user space.
- **A property test (slice 5).** For every `/Rotate` × view rotation ×
  CropBox offset × MediaBox not at the origin, `to_view ∘ from_view` is
  the identity within 1/16 pt.

### 6. Ops and primitives

#### 6.1 Ops: public, semantic, one schema

An **op** is a semantic edit as a caller states it ("crop pages 3–5").
Ops live in the new **`sheaf-edit`** crate. It holds types only (serde,
schemars, sheaf-core) and builds for wasm32-wasip2. sheaf-doc,
sheaf-plugin and sheaf-plugin-api depend on it.

**The types ops carry live in sheaf-edit too,** since it can't depend on
sheaf-doc. Slice 11 moves them out of sheaf-doc, and sheaf-doc
re-exports them:
- the id newtypes `PageId`, `OutlineId` (model.rs:23, :26), `AnnotId`
  (annot.rs:25) and `FieldId` (forms/field.rs:19), with `BlobId` (§3.1)
  and `Local`;
- every payload spec: the mark kinds (from `AnnotKind`, annot.rs:72)
  and `Style`, `TextLayer` and `OcrWord` (text_layer.rs:44, :34),
  `HeaderFooterSpec` and `BatesSpec` (from stamps.rs:46),
  `WatermarkSpec` and `WatermarkContent` (from watermark.rs:41),
  `TextStyle` (model.rs:107), colors, boxes, label ranges and the rest.
  `DestView` is already in sheaf-core.

Inherent methods that need sheaf-doc become free functions there.

**Conventions:**
- Names are VerbNoun.
- Ops address things by reference only (§6.6).
- Patches are tri-state: `Patch<T> = Keep | Clear | Set(T)`.
- Every spec is `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`.
- **Versioning.** Persisted JSON (`sheaf edit` files and plugin
  submissions) is one envelope:
  `{ "v": "1.0", "label"?: string, "ops": [...] }`.
  - `label` names the version a `sheaf edit` file makes. A plugin's
    envelope omits it, since `submit` takes the label separately; one
    that has it is `Invalid`.
  - A reader refuses an unknown major or a newer minor.
  - Every field added after 1.0 is `#[serde(default)]`; a committed
    schemars snapshot test enforces it.

```rust
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Clone, Debug)]
#[serde(tag = "op", rename_all = "kebab-case", deny_unknown_fields)] #[non_exhaustive]
pub enum Op {
    // pages
    MovePages      { pages: PageSel, before: Option<PageRef> },
    RotatePages    { pages: PageSel, by: Turn },                       // relative here; absolute in the prim
    DeletePages    { pages: PageSel },
    DuplicatePages { pages: PageSel, #[serde(default)] locals: Vec<Local> },
    InsertPages    { from: PageSource, #[serde(default)] pages: Option<RangeSet<u32>> /* None = all */,
                     before: Option<PageRef>, #[serde(default)] bring: Bring, #[serde(default)] locals: Vec<Local> },
    ReplacePages   { pages: Vec<PageRef>, from: PageSource, #[serde(default)] with: Option<RangeSet<u32>>,
                     #[serde(default)] bring: Bring },
    SetPageBoxes   { pages: PageSel, crop: Patch<BoxSpec>, trim: Patch<BoxSpec>, bleed: Patch<BoxSpec>, art: Patch<BoxSpec> },
    SetPageLabels  { ranges: Option<Vec<LabelRange>> },
    // bookmarks
    AddBookmark    { parent: Option<BookmarkRef>, at: Sibling, title: String, target: Option<PageRef>,
                     view: Option<DestView>, color: Option<Color>, style: TextStyle, local: Option<Local> },
    PatchBookmarks { bookmarks: Vec<BookmarkRef>, patch: BookmarkPatch },
    MoveBookmarks  { bookmarks: Vec<BookmarkRef>, parent: Option<BookmarkRef>, at: Sibling, with_pages: bool },
    DeleteBookmarks{ bookmarks: Vec<BookmarkRef> },
    // annotations
    AddMark        { page: PageRef, mark: NewMark, reply_to: Option<AnnotRef>, local: Option<Local>,
                     #[serde(default)] imported: Option<Imported> },  // Cap::User (§6.8)
    AddConnector   { a: Anchor, b: Anchor, style: Style, #[serde(default)] locals: Vec<Local> },
    PatchMarks     { marks: Vec<AnnotRef>, patch: MarkPatch, #[serde(default)] imported: Option<Imported> },
    MoveMarks      { marks: Vec<AnnotRef>, by: Delta, to_page: Option<PageRef> },
    DeleteAnnotations { annots: Vec<AnnotRef> },                       // marks, others' comments, links — not widgets
    AdoptAnnotations  { annots: Vec<AnnotRef> },                       // others' comment → mark, same id and /NM
    // page content
    SetOcrText     { page: PageRef, layer: TextLayer },
    ClearOcrText   { pages: PageSel },                                // unsaved AND saved layers
    SetWatermark   { spec: WatermarkSpec, z: Z, scope: ScopeSel },     // replaces ours of the same z, and Acrobat-added
    RemoveWatermark{ z: Option<Z>, pages: PageSel, #[serde(default)] include_authored: bool },
    SetHeaderFooter{ spec: HeaderFooterSpec, scope: ScopeSel },
    RemoveHeaderFooter { pages: PageSel, #[serde(default)] include_authored: bool },
    FlattenAnnotations { pages: PageSel, which: FlattenSet },          // binder edit AND export step
}
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)] #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub enum Transform {                  // accepted only by ExportTx (§6.3): never committable
    Op(Op),
    KeepPages { pages: PageSel, outline: OutlinePolicy /* Headings | Promote | Drop */, labels: bool },
    RemoveMarkup { keep: MarkupKeep /* links, widgets */ },
    RemovePrivateData,                // every key in private::KEYS (§15), legacy inline markers included
}
pub enum PageSource { File { file: BlobSel, name: String /* the file name the user picked: stems and notices */ },
                      Blank { size: PageSize, count: u32 }, Images { images: Vec<BlobSel>, fit: Fit } }
pub struct Bring { pub marks: bool, pub comments: bool, pub links: bool, pub form: FormBring /* New | Drop */,
                   pub outline: OutlineBring /* None | Append | MergeByTitle */, pub labels: bool }   // Default: all, New
pub struct NewMark { pub kind: MarkKind, pub geometry: MarkGeometry, pub style: Style, pub contents: String,
                     pub author: Option<String> /* None → host */, pub asset: Option<BlobSel> /* stamp, attachment */ }
                     // NO id, dates, /NM or partner
pub struct Imported { pub nm: String, pub created: PdfDate, pub modified: PdfDate, pub author: String }
                     // an imported comment's own /NM, dates and /T, kept as today (import.rs:274–286)
pub enum Delta { Page(Vec2), View(Vec2) }
```

**The 21 old `Edit` variants map as follows:**

| Old variant | New op |
|---|---|
| Move, Rotate, Delete, Duplicate, Insert, Replace | same names; Insert and Replace take page sets |
| Outline Insert, InsertTree, Move, Edit, Delete | `AddBookmark` (trees use locals), `MoveBookmarks`, `PatchBookmarks`, `DeleteBookmarks` |
| `AnnotAdd` | `AddMark` |
| `AnnotUpdate` | `PatchMarks` / `MoveMarks` |
| `AnnotDelete`, `CommentsDelete` | `DeleteAnnotations` |
| `ConnectorAdd` | `AddConnector` |
| `Set/ClearTextLayers` | `SetOcrText` / `ClearOcrText` |
| `SetHeaderFooter` | `SetHeaderFooter` / `RemoveHeaderFooter` |
| `SetPageLabels` | `SetPageLabels` |
| `RebasePages` | **deleted**: its callers say what they mean |

UI macros stay macros that emit batches with locals: Set Status, Color
by Level, Bookmarks from Pages, calculator ticks and paste. They read
the model through `Tx::reader()` (§6.3), so what they computed from is
recorded.

#### 6.2 The primitive

```rust
pub struct Prim { pub key: Key, pub before: Option<Val>, pub after: Option<Val> }
#[derive(Clone)]
pub enum Val { Order(im::Vector<PageId>), Page(Arc<PageDef>), Geom(Arc<Geom>), PageAnnots(im::Vector<AnnotId>),
               Annot(AnnotRec), Field(Arc<FieldNode>), Form(Arc<FormDoc>), Bookmark(Arc<Bookmark>),
               OutlineRoots(im::Vector<OutlineId>), Labels(Option<Arc<[LabelSpec]>>), Generator(Arc<Generator>) }
impl DocState {
    /// Sets the slot; asserts `before` (ptr_eq, else Eq); keeps the derived indexes current.
    pub fn apply(&mut self, p: &Prim) -> Result<(), Mismatch>;
}
```

- **Before-values cost nothing.** They are the parent's Arcs, and an im
  clone is O(1).
- **What before-values buy:** replay checking, `classify` from the step
  alone, the commit-time value check, and describe text for pruned
  parents.
- **Coalescing.** At commit and at amend, the prims are coalesced per
  key into one prim from the first `before` to the last `after`, and
  identity prims are dropped. A batch that sets `a` from x to y and back
  to x therefore leaves nothing (§8.5).

#### 6.3 Transactions, lowering and reads

A **transaction** (`Tx`) collects ops against a base version.
**Lowering** turns each op into prims inside it.

```rust
pub struct Tx {                                  // binder transaction: Op only; committable. Send + 'static.
    base: VersionId, base_state: DocState,       // what the caller saw (VersionId names that exact state)
    state: DocState,                             // working copy: read-your-writes
    ops: Vec<ResolvedOp>, prims: Vec<Prim>,
    seen: BTreeMap<Key, Option<Val>>,            // lowering reads: the value at first read
    decided: BTreeMap<Key, Option<Val>>,         // decision reads: what the caller based its ops on
    steps: Vec<StepDraft>, locals: LocalMap, created: Created, handed_out: bool,
    notices: Vec<Notice>, pins: Vec<Blob>, cx: Arc<LowerCx>,
}
pub struct ExportTx { /* the same machinery; accepts Transform; consumed only by export::run; never committed */ }
pub struct LowerCx { store: Arc<Store>, alloc: Arc<IdAlloc>, host: HostAttrs, settings: Arc<EditSettings>,
                     actor: Actor, grants: Grants /* plugin asset handles, CLI --with names */ }
impl Document { pub fn lower_cx(&self, actor: Actor) -> Arc<LowerCx>; }   // the only constructor: actors can't be forged
pub struct HostAttrs { pub person: Arc<str>, pub initials: Arc<str>, pub now: PdfDate }
impl Tx {
    pub fn apply(&mut self, op: Op, p: &Progress) -> Result<OpResult, EditError>;          // atomic per op
    pub fn apply_batch(&mut self, ops: Vec<Op>, p: &Progress) -> Result<Vec<OpResult>, EditError>;  // atomic
    pub fn check(&self, op: &Op) -> Result<(), EditError>;     // resolve refs + caps only: no puts, cascades or ids
    pub fn read(&mut self, key: Key);                           // decision read of the base value
    pub fn read_render(&mut self, page: PageId, inputs: &ViewInputs);  // the keys feeding what that page draws
    pub fn reader(&mut self) -> Reader<'_>;                     // model reads that record decision reads
    pub fn pin(&mut self, b: Blob);
    pub fn mark_handed_out(&mut self);                          // created ids were returned to a caller (plugins)
    pub fn state(&self) -> &DocState;
    pub fn preview(&self) -> Preview;                           // lines, effects, consequences vs saved, created, caps
}
```

**`Tx::apply` runs in three stages:**
1. **Resolve.** `BlobSel` becomes a `BlobRef` through the Store, the
   CLI's `--with` names or the plugin's asset grants. A raw
   `BlobSel::Id` from a plugin actor is `Refused`.
2. **Check capabilities.** The cap of the resolved op and its targets is
   checked against `cx.actor` (§6.7) **before** any lowering.
3. **Lower.**

**Lowering reads only** the `Tx` state, immutable `Src` facts, the
Store, `HostAttrs` (captured once per `Tx`) and the allocator. It never
reads Document side tables, paths, caches or engines. Replay doesn't
re-run it, so purity is a hygiene rule; the prims are what replay
trusts.

**An op reads only the keys whose values it uses.** A target's existence
is checked by the before-value of what the op writes, not by a separate
read. `SetPageBoxes` reads and writes `Geom(p)` alone, so an OCR flush,
which writes `Page(p)`, never conflicts with a crop dialog.

**Two kinds of reads are recorded, both by value:**
- **Lowering reads** come from the accessors, cascades included.
  Resolving `PageSel::Positions` or `PageRef::Pos` reads `Order`.
- **Decision reads** are what the caller based its ops on. Each records
  the value it saw:
  - `Tx::read(key)`;
  - `Tx::reader()`, used by UI macros and the plugin host. A key the Tx
    already wrote is its own value and isn't recorded;
  - host-resolved payloads: M10's calculated values, OCR words, trim
    boxes. `FormsHost::resolve` returns `(payload, reads)`;
  - `Tx::read_render(page, inputs)`, for anything derived from pixels or
    page text. It records the keys that feed what is drawn under those
    inputs. The box and rotation the render used are not reads: a
    consumer that converts pixels keeps the render transform with its
    result (§5). Under `ViewInputs::content_only()` that is `Page(p)`
    alone.

**The host resolve stage** is the general pattern for work that needs
engines before lowering:

```
resolve(&Snapshot, &EnginePool, &Progress) -> (resolved ops, decision reads)  →  pure lowering in a Tx
```

OCR, Trim White, form calculation and M11's partial-image redaction all
follow it.

**Progress and cancel** reach lowering, because 7,000-page ops
(`FlattenAnnotations`, `RemoveHeaderFooter`'s filters) report per page.
A canceled `apply` leaves the Tx as it was before that op.

#### 6.4 Validation: fail, never repair

- **Every reference must resolve:** pages (including Move, Rotate,
  Delete, Duplicate and `ClearOcrText`, which ignore unknown ids today),
  `before`, bookmark targets, label anchors, generator scopes, reply
  parents, connector partners, locals and blobs.
- **Errors are typed:**

  ```
  EditError::{ NotFound{op, what}, Invalid{op, why}, Ambiguous{op, what}, Content{op, page, why},
               Conflict{since, keys}, Refused(Cap), GroupEnded, NotReady, Cancelled, Mismatch{version},
               Internal{what} }
  ```

  `Internal` is a bug in sheaf, such as a cascade that doesn't settle
  (§6.5). It panics in debug builds. M10 slice 9 adds
  `Locked { field }` for fields a signature locks.

- There is no lenient mode. The app prunes its own selections on every
  ChangeSet.
- **`DocState::check_invariants()`** runs after every op in tests, debug
  builds and the corpus run. It checks:
  - `order` ⇔ `pages` ⇔ `geoms` keys agree;
  - every id in `page_annots[p]` has `annots[id].page == p`;
  - widget ↔ field ↔ terminal lists agree;
  - no cycles or orphans in outline or field kids;
  - scopes and frozen maps name live pages;
  - at most one sheaf generator per kind and z, `/NM` unique per page,
    and field full names unique;
  - every `BlobRef` and `Src` resolves;
  - no ContentStack references an `AnnotId` or `FieldId`.

#### 6.5 Normalization: cascades declared once

A **cascade** is a named rule that keeps the state consistent after an
op (deleting a page deletes its annotations). Rules are pure, idempotent
and named. They run on the keys an op wrote, to a fixed point (more than
8 rounds is `EditError::Internal`). Their output is ordinary prims in
the same step. Each is reported as `Cascade { rule, objects }`, which
feeds notices such as "Deleted 3 pages and 2 bookmarks — Undo".

| Rule | Trigger | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `page.annots` | page removed | its annotations removed (marks, others', links, widgets) |
| `annot.thread` | annotation removed | replies and statuses whose `reply_to` is it, transitively |
| `annot.partner` | connector removed | partner's `partner` cleared |
| `widget.field` | widget removed | a terminal whose last modeled widget went (and `outside == 0`) is removed; empty parents pruned; `/CO` and `roots` pruned (**deliberate change**: the model test `form_fields_follow_their_pages` (model.rs:3055; the assert at :3080) inverts, so the model matches what `keep_only` writes) |
| `page.bookmarks` | page removed | bookmarks targeting it removed, children promoted |
| `page.labels` | page removed | a range anchored there re-anchors to the next surviving page of its range, else is dropped (**deliberate change**) |
| `page.generators` | page removed | dropped from `Pages` scopes, with its frozen Bates entry; numbers are never reused |

**Op-specific lowering:**
- **DuplicatePages.**
  - The copy gets `copy + 1` and joins every `Pages` scope its original
    is in. It gets no frozen Bates number, and its OCR layers are shared
    by Arc.
  - Marks get fresh ids, with threads remapped inside the copied set.
  - Others' annotations get fresh ids and `copy + 1`. A deleted comment
    isn't in the list, so it can't reappear.
  - Widgets get fresh ids, and their fields get a new `FormInst` per
    source instance. The new instance inherits its original's name in
    `FormDoc.names`, and its fields are renamed apart under that name's
    stem, with `Notice::CopiesRenamed`. A merged widget stays merged in
    its copy.
  - The `/StructParents` and `/StructParent` rule is the emitter's
    (§11.3).
- **ReplacePages.**
  - The slot keeps its PageId, and `Geom` comes from the new base.
  - The old base's others' annotations and widgets go through the rules.
    Marks stay, in PageSpace. Filters and static layers are dropped.
  - Extra originals are deleted and extra replacements inserted.
    Incoming fields follow InsertPages.
- **InsertPages.**
  - Facts are read from the source, for the selected pages only.
  - Incoming marks get fresh ids. A connector keeps its partner only if
    the partner came too.
  - The outline is merged by title or appended.
  - **Fields.** One new `FormInst` per op, with `PageSource::File.name`
    recorded in `FormDoc.names`. If any incoming node's name clashes,
    the whole instance goes under a new parent named for that name's
    stem (`stem`, `stem_2`, …), and the decision is recorded in the
    prims. `/DR` font renames are recorded in `FormDoc`.
  - **Notices** go into `Commit.notices`, with the exact texts ported
    from M10 slices 4–5 (`form_notice`, model.rs:845):
    - `FormRenamed { under, file, scripted }`: "fields from {file} are
      under “{under}”, since their names were taken", plus "; its
      scripts still use the old names" when scripted;
    - `CopiesRenamed { under, scripted }`, from DuplicatePages: "the
      copies' fields are under “{under}”, apart from the originals",
      with the same suffix;
    - `SignatureNotValidHere { file }`: "the signature from {file} is no
      longer valid here" (the signed fields are kept with their values);
    - `ScriptsOff`: "scripts are off: fields from more than one form
      have them".

    One commit's notices are capitalized and joined with ". ", as
    today. Tests pin them: forms_merge.rs:196–210 and :298–311, and
    forms_saves.rs:306–336 (sheaf-ops).
- **KeepPages (Headings).** Before the `Order` put, bookmarks whose
  target goes but which keep surviving descendants are retargeted to
  `None`. That is extract's "parent becomes heading"; DeletePages keeps
  "promote".
- **SetWatermark and SetHeaderFooter.**
  - They replace an existing sheaf generator of the same kind and z in
    the same step.
  - They set `replaces` to their kind, so Acrobat-added artifacts of
    that kind on covered pages are dropped at emit (§11.4), pages
    inserted later included.
  - Lowering is O(1) and reads no content facts.
  - Authored pagination artifacts (a Word or InDesign running head) are
    never touched.
- **RemoveWatermark and RemoveHeaderFooter.**
  - They remove our generator (or narrow its scope).
  - They add `DropMarked`/`DropStreams` filters for unbacked sheaf
    blocks and Acrobat-added blocks on the selected pages.
  - With `include_authored`, they also remove authored artifacts.
  - The command runs as a job whose resolve phase forces the `blocks`
    facts, and its describe line counts each origin: "Remove the header
    and footer from pages 1–20 (2 made in Acrobat; 12 the author's left
    in place)".

#### 6.6 References and selectors

```rust
pub enum PageRef     { Id(PageId), New(Local), Pos(u32) }                   // 17 | "$3" | {"pos": 3}
pub enum BookmarkRef { Id(OutlineId), New(Local), Path(Vec<String>) }       // 9 | "$1" | {"path": ["Part I", "Ch. 2"]}
pub enum AnnotRef    { Id(AnnotId), New(Local), Nm { nm: String, page: Option<PageRef> } }   // {"nm": "…", "page": {...}}
pub enum BlobSel     { Id(BlobId), With(String), Asset(u32) }               // {"blob": "b…"} | {"with": "logo"} | {"asset": 2}
pub struct Local(pub u16);
pub enum PageSel { Ids(Vec<PageRef>), All, Span { first: PageRef, last: PageRef },
                   Positions(RangeSet<u32>), Bookmark(BookmarkRef) }        // #[non_exhaustive]
pub enum ScopeSel { All /* includes pages added later */, Pages(PageSel) }
```

**Per-kind references** have a custom deserializer with clear errors. A
misspelled local is an error, not a fallthrough to another variant. An
ambiguous `Path` or `Nm` (two matches) is `Ambiguous`.

**Positions are 1-based,** as the UI shows them: `pos` and `Positions`,
and the source pages of `InsertPages.pages` and `ReplacePages.with`.
The schemars docs say so, and a CLI golden pins it. (`PageBase.index`
is internal and 0-based.)

**Positions resolve against the pre-op state,** meaning the Tx state
when that op runs. This matches reads, describe lines and the intuition
of a sequential script: `[delete-pages 1, rotate-pages 2]` rotates the
page now in position 2. The step stores the resolved ids. The app never
sends positions: dialogs resolve indexes to ids against the version they
opened on, so their transactions don't read `Order` at all.

**Locals.** A **local** names something an op creates, for later ops in
the same transaction. A creating op names its results with
`local`/`locals`, and a later op in the same `Tx` uses `New(n)`. An
undeclared, duplicate or wrong-kind local is `Invalid`. `Commit.created`
maps every local, and every implicit creation in order, to real ids.

**Id guessing this deletes:**
- "the last mark on the page" (docview.rs:1965, :2476);
- `skip(before)` for paste (markup_tools.rs:1684);
- by `/NM` for adoption (adopt.rs:87);
- the next bookmark in pre-order (outline_panel.rs:206);
- pages after the old count in combine;
- `reveal_new_bookmarks`' outline diff (docview.rs:5876).

**`BlobSel::With` and `Asset`** exist because the CLI
(`--with NAME=PATH`) and plugins (`assets.upload`) are their producers.
They are resolved before lowering, and the step stores the `BlobRef`.

#### 6.7 Capabilities and actors

```rust
pub enum Cap { Plugin, Approval, User }
pub enum Actor { User, Plugin { id: Arc<str>, version: Arc<str> }, Cli, Job(JobKind /* Ocr | Trim | Import | Insert */) }
```

**The cap is computed from the resolved op and the kinds of its
targets,** not from the op's name alone. A test checks that the cap
table is total over (op, target kind), and that the actor table covers
every actor.

| Cap | Ops and targets |
|---|---|
