| `Plugin` | Move, Rotate, Delete, Duplicate pages; `SetPageLabels`; all bookmark ops; `AddMark` (assets only through grants), `PatchMarks`, `MoveMarks`, `AddConnector`; `DeleteAnnotations` whose targets are all sheaf marks |
| `Approval` | `SetPageBoxes`, `SetWatermark`, `RemoveWatermark`, `SetHeaderFooter`, `RemoveHeaderFooter`, `ClearOcrText`, `AdoptAnnotations`, `FlattenAnnotations`; `DeleteAnnotations` touching others' comments or links |
| `User` | `InsertPages`, `ReplacePages` (host-prepared sources), `SetOcrText` (the OCR job); `AddMark` and `PatchMarks` with `imported` |

**What each actor may submit:**

| Actor | May submit |
|---|---|
| `User`, `Cli` | every op |
| `Job(Import)`, `Job(Insert)` | every op (they hold `User`) |
| `Job(Ocr)` | `SetOcrText` and `ClearOcrText` only |
| `Job(Trim)` | `SetPageBoxes` only |
| `Plugin` | `Plugin` ops, plus what its manifest grants (nothing in 0.4) |

- **Only `ExportTx` accepts `Transform`, so exports are export-only by
  type.** `Document::commit` takes a `Tx`, which can't hold one. M11's
  "plugins never apply redactions" is therefore enforced by the types.
  If M11 later moves an apply-in-place into `Op`, that op gets
  `Cap::User`.
- **Plugins in 0.4 get `Plugin` caps only.** `Approval` ops need a
  manifest permission, and none is granted in 0.4. The agent is a later
  milestone (§20).
- **Provenance is session-only.** History shows the actor ("Plugin Tax
  Bookmarks: …"). Nothing goes into the file except a mark's `/T`, which
  comes from the host.

#### 6.8 Host-owned attributes and identity

**Lowering sets these identically for every surface,** and never takes
them from callers:
- `AnnotId` and `/NM`;
- `created` and `modified`, from `HostAttrs.now` (one date per `Tx`);
- the default author;
- the tokens `<<name>>`, `<<initials>>`, `<<date>>` and `<<time>>` in
  contents and stamp text;
- clamps (opacity, stroke) and kind filters (fill only on fillable
  kinds, cloudy only on cloudable kinds);
- tape defaults and connector partners.

`NewMark` and `MarkPatch` have no fields for any of these. `sign_marks`,
`keep_unseen` and `annot_from_wit`'s repairs are deleted. `PatchMarks`
and `MoveMarks` apply only the fields they name, to the record as it is
when lowered.

**Imported comments are the one exception.** Comment import keeps each
comment's `/NM`, dates and author, as today, so a second import of the
same XFDF finds its marks by `AnnotRef::Nm` and replies still thread by
name. `AddMark` and `PatchMarks` take them as `imported: Imported`
(§6.1), which needs `Cap::User`, so only the import job and the CLI can
pass it. On `PatchMarks`, `imported.nm` must equal the mark's `/NM`. A
comment whose `/NM` names another tool's comment here is
`AdoptAnnotations` followed by that `PatchMarks`.

**A plugin never learns the user's identity** (proposal 20).
- When the actor is a plugin without the `identity` manifest permission,
  `<<name>>` and `<<initials>>` stay literal.
- Marks a plugin creates are authored by the plugin's name.
- `annot-view.author` reports a role (`you`, `other`, `plugin <id>`)
  instead of a name.
- The `identity` permission exists, but no bundled plugin asks for it.

### 7. Effects

#### 7.1 `classify`: the one place that knows what a change means

```rust
bitflags! { pub struct Flags: u32 {
    const PAGE_ADD = 1<<0; const PAGE_REMOVE = 1<<1; const PAGE_ORDER = 1<<2; const GEOMETRY = 1<<3;
    const CONTENT = 1<<4;          // a layer or generator output added or changed (visible inputs)
    const REMOVES = 1<<5;          // content-bearing data that existed is gone
    const REPLACES_TEXT = 1<<6;    // a text-bearing value replaced or cleared (contents, /RC, struck text, tape rows,
                                   // /T, title, label prefix, field value non-empty → other, OCR words, recipe text)
    const LIVE_REDRAW = 1<<7;      // a generator's live tokens re-laid out under an unchanged recipe (appendable)
    const ANNOTS = 1<<8; const FIELD_VALUES = 1<<9; const FIELD_TREE = 1<<10; const FORM = 1<<11;
    const OUTLINE = 1<<12; const LABELS = 1<<13;
}}
bitflags! { pub struct SigClass: u8 { const FILL = 1; const ANNOT = 2; const OTHER = 4; } }   // M12 adds SIGN, LTV
pub struct Effects { pub flags: Flags, pub sig: SigClass, pub pages: PageTouch, pub keys: Arc<KeySet> }
pub fn classify(key: &Key, before: Option<&Val>, after: Option<&Val>, cx: &ClassifyCx) -> Effects;  // pure, total
```

**Effects** are what a change means for saving, signatures, rendering
and the app. `SigClass` is a **set**, not a ladder. DocMDP and UR3
permissions are allow masks over it (§9.3), so M12's SIGN and LTV
classes fit without reordering anything.

| Change | Flags | Sig |
|---|---|---|
| `Annot` None → Mark or Foreign | ANNOTS | ANNOT |
| `Annot` Some → None (mark, foreign, link) | ANNOTS \| REMOVES | ANNOT |
| Mark contents, struck text, tape rows or kind changed | ANNOTS \| REPLACES_TEXT | ANNOT |
| Mark geometry or style only | ANNOTS | ANNOT |
| `PageAnnots` membership or order delta of marks and others' annotations | ANNOTS | ANNOT |
| `PageAnnots` membership delta of widgets | FIELD_TREE | OTHER |
| `Geom` changed | GEOMETRY | OTHER |
| `Order` changed | PAGE_ORDER (+ PAGE_ADD / PAGE_REMOVE by diff) | OTHER |
| `Page` content: over-layer added | CONTENT | OTHER |
| `Page` content: filter added, or a layer that existed removed or changed | CONTENT \| REMOVES (OCR words: \| REPLACES_TEXT) | OTHER |
| `Field` value None or empty → v | FIELD_VALUES | FILL |
| `Field` value v → v′ | FIELD_VALUES \| REPLACES_TEXT | FILL |
| `Field` node added, removed, renamed or reparented | FIELD_TREE (removed: \| REMOVES) | OTHER |
| Widget `/AS`, or appearance inputs derived from the value, only | FIELD_VALUES | FILL |
| Widget rect, `/MK`, `/DA` or flags | FIELD_TREE | OTHER |
| `Form` changed | FORM | OTHER |
| `Generator` added | CONTENT over scope | OTHER |
| `Generator` added with `replaces`, where covered pages of the saved file hold Acrobat-added artifacts of those kinds | CONTENT \| REMOVES | OTHER |
| `Generator` recipe text changed or removed where it was drawn in the saved file | REMOVES \| REPLACES_TEXT | OTHER |
| `Generator` live tokens re-laid out, same recipe | LIVE_REDRAW | OTHER |
| `Bookmark` title changed | OUTLINE \| REPLACES_TEXT | OTHER |
| `Bookmark` removed | OUTLINE \| REMOVES | OTHER |
| `Labels` prefix that disappears | LABELS \| REPLACES_TEXT | OTHER |

`ClassifyCx` gives `classify` the layout context (positions before and
after, `needs()`) and a pure accessor to source facts (`blocks`, for the
`replaces` row). It is still a pure function of its inputs.

#### 7.2 Aggregation, judged by content against the saved version

- **Per step,** the union of prim effects is stored (`Step.effects`).
  Previews, approvals and ChangeSets use it.
- **For a save,** effects are **re-judged** against the saved version.
  - `path_keys(saved, target)` is the union of `Version.keys` along the
    tree path saved → LCA → target. When the saved version has been
    pruned (§8.7), the saved anchor's `keys_to_root` joins the union.
  - For each key in it, `classify(key, saved_value, target_value)` runs
    once, with `ptr_eq` first.
- **The result is content-based.** Replaced mark text, a renamed
  bookmark, a changed header text, a replaced field value, a replaced
  OCR layer and a deleted mark from an inserted file all count. Undoing
  back to the saved version empties the path.
- **This replaces** `removes_content`, `annots_dirty_pages`,
  `fields_dirty`, `header_footer_dirty`, `outline_dirty`,
  `labels_dirty`, `form_changed`, the five `saved_*` copies and
  `baked_header_footer`.

**An Append's emission scope is closed over its dependencies.**
`emit_deps(key, saved, target) -> ObjKeySet` is a registry-rule
function, exhaustive over `Key`. The Append scope is the closure of
`path_keys` under it.

| Key | Also emits |
|---|---|
| `Order` | changed `/Pages` nodes; `/PageLabels` when labels exist; pages whose links target a page whose written-ness changed (a links-by-target index); pages covered by a live-token generator whose layout digest ≠ `SaveRecord.drawn` |
| `Geom(p)` | `p`'s page dict; generator blocks on `p` whose layout changed |
| `Page(p)` | `p`'s page dict, `/Contents`, blocks, `PageRes` |
| `PageAnnots(p)` | `p`'s page dict (`/Annots`) |
| `Annot(a)` | its annotation objects (`AnnotPart`s); for a merged widget, its field object |
| `Field(f)` | its dict (or merged widget); its widgets' appearances when the value changed; the parent's `/Kids`; AcroForm `/Fields` for a root |
| `Form` | the AcroForm owned keys; appearances of widgets that inherit `/DA` or `/DR` |
| `Bookmark(b)` | its item; its parent's `/First`, `/Last`, `/Count`; its neighbors' `/Prev`, `/Next` |
| `OutlineRoots` | the Outlines root and the first and last items |
| `Labels` | `/PageLabels`; pages with `<<label>>` tokens whose layout changed |
| `Generator(g)` | `/SheafGenerators`; blocks on covered pages whose layout changed; those pages' dicts |

A property test in the default run checks the closure: after every save
in a random sequence of ops, Appends, Rewrites, Save As, undo, redo and
jump, the reopen facts of the written file equal the reopen facts of a
fresh Rewrite of the same state (Tests, fixtures and gates).

#### 7.3 Consumers

| Consumer | Uses |
|---|---|
| Save planner and its notice ("Rewriting: the text of 2 comments changed since the last save") | `SavePlan` |
| Signed-file prompt | `SigImpact`, `AppendAnyway` |
| Plugin approval and dialog consequences | `Preview.effects`, `Preview.consequences` in words |
| Menus (`can_apply`) | `Tx::check` (no lowering) |
| History panel | a badge on steps with `REMOVES` |
| M11 leak check | over the **output** (§20), not DocState |

### 8. History

#### 8.1 Versions are immutable states

```rust
pub struct Step {
    pub op: Arc<ResolvedOp>,      // selectors → ids, locals → ids, BlobSel → BlobRef: what describe and merge read
    pub prims: Arc<[Prim]>,       // AUTHORITATIVE for replay; dropped once the version becomes the root
    pub effects: Effects,
    pub line: Line,               // describe(), against the state before this op (recomputed on a merged commit)
    pub cascades: Arc<[Cascade]>,
}
pub struct Version {
    pub parent: Option<VersionId>, pub children: Vec<VersionId>, pub redo_to: Option<VersionId>,
    pub label: Arc<str>, pub actor: Actor, pub at: PdfDate,
    pub steps: Vec<Step>, pub keys: Arc<KeySet>,   // union over steps: save paths, ChangeSets
    pub state: Option<DocState>, pub snapshot_bytes: usize, pub step_bytes: usize,
    pub group: Option<GroupId>, pub sealed: u16,   // > 0 while a save of this version is in flight
    pub saves: Vec<SaveNote>,                      // { path, at, strategy } of every save of this version (History panel)
    pub record: Option<Arc<SaveRecord>>,           // kept whole only while this is the saved version (§9.1)
}
```

- **A VersionId names one state, forever.** Nothing mutates a version's
  state.
- **An amend replaces the version** with a new id under the same parent.
  The parent's `children` and `redo_to` are updated, and the old id
  stops resolving.
- **The snapshot tree is otherwise unchanged:** push, `redo_to`, jump,
  timeline, branch, `MAX_VERSIONS`, and `HistoryPolicy` (200 snapshots,
  256 MB).
- **Pinned snapshots.** `keeps_snapshot` covers the root, the cursor,
  the saved version and sealed versions.

#### 8.2 Replay: exact and checked

- `ensure_state(v)` replays prims from the nearest kept snapshot, and
  `apply` asserts every before-value.
- **A `Mismatch` during replay means corruption, an internal bug.** It
  surfaces as "History is damaged at version N" in the History panel,
  marks that version unreachable, and panics in debug builds. Undo never
  quietly does nothing.
- **Replay never re-runs lowering.** What `compute` reads today without
  recording it stops mattering: `page_count`, `page_size`, the rng,
  `Document.forms` and path stems.
- **The property test covers every op:** random batches over a
  corpus-built document, with groups, branches, undo, redo, jump, random
  trims and random byte-budget pruning. Rebuilt states equal the
  snapshots under a test-only deep `Eq`, with identical ids, and one
  corrupted before-value yields `Mismatch`.

#### 8.3 describe

- `describe(op, before: &DocState) -> Line` runs inside `Tx::apply`
  against that op's pre-state, so later ops in a batch print the right
  page numbers.
- When a commit merges onto a moved cursor, the lines are recomputed
  against the merged pre-state (cheap), so "Crop page 5" is still true
  after a concurrent reorder.
- Lines are specific: "Crop pages 3–5", "Add watermark “DRAFT” behind
  all pages", "Delete pages 4–6 and 2 bookmarks". A batch of 7,000 ops
  of one kind collapses into one line.
- `VersionView`, approval cards and plugin labels read stored lines.
  describe.rs's 21-arm match goes.

#### 8.4 Groups: one undo step per job or call

A **group** makes the versions of one job or plugin call one undo step.

```rust
pub enum GroupKind { Ocr, PluginCall { plugin: Arc<str> } }      // M10 slice 11 adds Typing
impl Document {
    pub fn begin_group(&mut self, kind: GroupKind) -> GroupId;
    pub fn end_group(&mut self, g: GroupId);
    pub fn rollback_group(&mut self, g: GroupId) -> Result<Option<ChangeSet>, EditError>;
    pub fn revert_group(&mut self, g: GroupId, label: &str) -> Result<Commit, EditError>;
}
```

**The amend rule.** A commit in group `g` amends the cursor version when
all of these hold:
- the cursor version is tagged `g`;
- it has no children;
- it has no `saves`;
- it is not sealed.

The amend coalesces the prims (§6.2), recomputes the version's byte
accounting and mints a new id. If the amended version nets to identity,
it is removed and the cursor moves to its parent. Any other commit in
`g` pushes a new version tagged `g`.

**`GroupEnded`** is returned exactly when the group's latest version is
not an ancestor-or-self of the cursor, meaning the user undid it or
jumped away. Undone results are never re-applied.

**Interleaving is allowed.** If the user edits during an OCR run, OCR's
next flush pushes a new version tagged `g`, shown as "Recognize text
(continued)". The group is then several fragments. Undo steps through
them one at a time, and the History panel's "Undo all of Recognize text"
calls `revert_group`.

**`revert_group`** commits the inverse prims of all of `g`'s versions,
latest first, with before and after swapped, as one new version. It
validates by value against the cursor (§8.5). On a conflict it fails,
nothing changes, and the user is told which later edit touched the same
data.

**`rollback_group`** (a plugin trap, Err or cancel):
- **Truncate** when the group's only version is the cursor and is
  childless, unsaved and unsealed. The cursor jumps to the pre-group
  parent and the version is removed, leaving no redo entry.
- **Otherwise revert,** as `revert_group`.
- **On a conflict,** keep the group's work and notify: "Plugin X failed;
  its partial edits were kept".

**Who uses groups in M10.5:** OCR (a flush every 10 pages or 2 s) and
plugin calls (§14.3). Dialogs and drags don't. They preview with
`Tx::preview` and commit once (§8.6).

#### 8.5 Commit, preview and stale bases

A **stale base** is a transaction built on a version that is no longer
the cursor.

```rust
pub enum Base { Current, At(VersionId) }
pub enum Stale { Validate, Relower }
pub struct CommitOpts { pub label: Option<Arc<str>>, pub group: Option<GroupId>, pub base: Base, pub stale: Stale }
pub struct Commit { pub version: Option<VersionId> /* None: no-op */, pub amended: bool, pub created: Created,
                    pub change: ChangeSet, pub effects: Effects, pub lines: Arc<[Line]>,
                    pub cascades: Arc<[Cascade]>, pub notices: Vec<Notice> }
impl Document {
    pub fn tx(&self, actor: Actor) -> Tx;                          // base = cursor
    pub fn tx_at(snap: &Snapshot, cx: Arc<LowerCx>) -> Tx;         // off-thread; base = snap.version
    pub fn commit(&mut self, tx: Tx, opts: CommitOpts) -> Result<Commit, EditError>;
    pub fn undo(&mut self) -> Option<ChangeSet>; pub fn redo(&mut self) -> Option<ChangeSet>;
    pub fn jump(&mut self, v: VersionId) -> Option<ChangeSet>;
    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> Snapshot;                            // O(1): instance, version, state, store
    pub fn save_input(&mut self, v: VersionId) -> SaveInput;       // seals v; computes path_keys (§9.1)
    pub fn record_save(&mut self, rec: SaveRecord) -> ChangeSet;   // on rec.version; unseals
    pub fn abandon_save(&mut self, v: VersionId);                  // a failed or canceled save: unseals
}
```

**The commit rule is one algorithm:**

```
if tx.base == cursor:
    new state = tx.state                      // exact: VersionIds name immutable states
else:
    for (key, seen) in tx.decided: cursor[key] ≡ seen, else Conflict    // ptr_eq, then Eq
    match stale:
      Validate:
        for (key, seen) in tx.seen ∪ {first before of each written key}: cursor[key] ≡ seen, else Conflict
        new state = cursor ⊕ tx.prims        // apply re-asserts before-values; a failure is Conflict, never Mismatch
        re-run the cascade and uniqueness rules over the written keys on the new state;
        any extra prim or violation → Conflict
      Relower:
        tx' = lower(tx.ops, cursor, pinned ids)     // a lowering error is returned as is (e.g. the anchor page is gone)
        if tx.handed_out and tx'.created ≠ tx.created → Conflict
        new state = tx'.state;  prims, lines = tx'.prims, tx'.lines
coalesce prims per key; drop identities; none left → no-op (version: None)
amend if eligible (§8.4), else push;  debug: check_invariants on the touched ids
```

- **Validation by value replaces unions of path keys.**
  - It is exact under amends, because a version id never hides a changed
    state.
  - Changes that cancel out don't conflict.
  - A pruned base doesn't matter.
- **The post-merge cascade and uniqueness check closes phantoms.**
  Uniqueness covers one sheaf generator per kind and z, `/NM` per page
  and field full names, on the touched ids only. For example, a stale
  `DeletePages` while another transaction added a generator scoped to
  that page would leave a dangling scope; the check turns it into a
  Conflict.
- **`Relower` handles phantoms by construction,** because the cascades
  run on the cursor.
- **Policies:**

| Caller | Base | Stale | Decision reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| Gestures, menus, paste | Current | — | — |
| Dialogs (crop, watermark, header/footer, labels…) | the version opened on | Validate | none beyond lowering (positions were resolved to ids at open) |
| `insert_file`, blank and image inserts, comment import | the planning snapshot | Relower | none: what they planned from are source facts (immutable) |
| Blank review deletes | the planning snapshot | Relower | `Page(p)` of each page the user confirmed (what its thumbnail showed) |
| Trim White | the planning snapshot | Validate | `read_render(p, content_only)` of each measured page, which is `Page(p)` |
| OCR flush | the run's snapshot per page | Validate | `read_render(p, content_only)` taken at render time, which is `Page(p)`: a crop or rotate during the run never conflicts |
| Plugin call (§14.3) | the call's first read | Relower | every WIT read; `created` must match |
| M10 form calculations (FormsHost) | the cascade's snapshot | Validate | the calculation's inputs |

- **Re-lowering pins ids,** so ids already handed to a plugin stay
  valid. If re-lowering would hand out different ids, the commit is a
  Conflict instead.
- **Preview ids come from the real `IdAlloc`.** A committed preview has
  the same render keys, so its tiles are reused.
- **`Snapshot` is O(1).** `SaveInput` (§9.1) is computed only on the
  save path, so snapshots carry no dirty flags.

#### 8.6 Previews

- **What `Tx::preview()` returns:** the scratch state, lines, effects,
  consequences, created ids and caps.
- **Where dialog previews run.** Previews lower on a background executor
  with a generation token. Stale results are dropped.
- **Previews follow the document.** A dialog re-lowers its preview on
  every `ChangeSet`, so a dialog left open across an undo shows the
  current state.
- **Rendering.** The realizer renders the scratch state's touched pages
  (§10.1), so the crop, header/footer and watermark dialogs show the
  page as PDFium will draw it.
- **Drags.** GPUI draws a ghost, and `ViewInputs.dragging` hides the
  originals from the realization. Mouse-up makes one commit,
  `MoveMarks { by }`, applied to the record as it is at commit, so a
  concurrent plugin or OCR change survives.

#### 8.7 The memory budget

- **Trimming snapshots** frees `snapshot_bytes` only. That is the first
  response to `HistoryPolicy` pressure.
- **Pruning versions** frees `step_bytes`. When
  `step_bytes + blob_bytes` still exceed the byte budget after trimming,
  versions are pruned in this order:
  1. off-line branches, oldest first;
  2. then the oldest versions on the line, advancing the root.
- **The root may pass the saved version.** When it does, a **detached
  saved anchor** `{ state, record, keys_to_root }` keeps what the
  planner needs, and `keys_to_root` grows as the root advances. A
  document whose anchor is gone (never saved) plans `NoSaveRecord` and
  Rewrites.
- **When a version becomes the root,** its steps drop their prims and
  keep their lines and effects, since nothing replays into the root.
- **Sealed and saved versions are never pruned.**

Under this policy, 200 re-OCRs of a 1,000-page scan (about 2.4 GB of
text layers if all were kept) stay within the budget: history gets
shorter instead of RSS growing.

### 9. Saves

#### 9.1 Saves are outputs

```rust
pub struct SaveRecord {
    pub version: VersionId, pub path: Arc<Path>, pub at: PdfDate, pub strategy: StrategyKind,
    pub stamp: FileStamp,            // len, mtime, xxh3 of the last 64 KiB, xxh3 of the 4 KiB at the last startxref target
    pub content_hash: ContentHash,   // xxh3-128 of the whole file: computed while writing, lazily for an opened
                                     // file, and extended by each Append (the record keeps the streaming state)
    pub xref: XrefFacts,             // kind (table | stream), start, size, /ID, header offset
    pub crypt: Option<Arc<Crypt>>,
    pub map: Arc<ObjMap>,            // ObjKey → (num, gen) for every object sheaf WROTE into this file
    pub present: Arc<BitSet>,        // primary-source object numbers live in this file (keep_only pruned the rest)
    pub roots: Arc<RootValues>,      // the written values of UNOWNED keys of Catalog, AcroForm, Outlines root, Names roots
    pub tree: Arc<TreeShape>,        // the file's /Pages nodes, kids, and each node's size at open
    pub drawn: Arc<HashMap<(PageId, GenId), (u128 /* layout */, u128 /* recipe */)>>,
    pub transforms: Arc<[SaveTransform]>,       // what save policy already did to this file
    pub pending_tail: Option<PendingTail>,      // a restored document's recovery sections not yet on disk (§11.10)
}
pub struct PendingTail { pub bytes: Arc<[u8]>, pub removes: bool /* the sections removed or replaced saved text */ }
pub struct SaveInput { pub target: DocState, pub saved: Option<SavedSide>, pub path_keys: Arc<KeySet> }
pub struct SavedSide { pub state: DocState, pub record: Arc<SaveRecord> }
```

A **save record** says what sheaf knows about the file it last wrote:
where every object sheaf wrote sits, which original objects are still
there, and what policy already did to it. It drives the next save.

**On open,** the opened file's record is synthesized:
- an identity map;
- `present` set to every object number in use;
- `roots` from the source's catalog-level objects;
- `tree` from the source;
- `drawn` from the `/SheafBlock` markers;
- `transforms` empty.

The first incremental save is therefore not a special case.

**A save:**
1. `save_input(V)` seals V.
2. Plan in the background (§9.2), and ask any `Ask`s.
3. `materialize` in the background.
4. `record_save` sets `Document.saved = V`, sets V's `record` (reducing
   the previous saved version's record to its `SaveNote`), appends a
   `SaveNote`, unseals V, and handles the recovery sidecar (§11.10).

No version, state or source changes. Dirty is `saved != cursor`, revert
is a jump, and undo across saves is exact.

**Edits during a save are safe.**
- V is sealed, so an OCR flush or a later typing commit can't amend it.
  They push a child version, which stays dirty and goes into the next
  save.
- `rollback_group`, pruning and `remove_subtree` never remove a sealed
  or saved version.
- A second save waits for the first, because its base is the first
  save's file.

**An Unchanged plan** (the path's effects are empty, for example after
an undo back to the saved state by a different route) writes nothing. It
attaches the current record to the cursor, so `saved = cursor` and the
document is clean.

**The next Append** writes the emission of the closure of
`path_keys(saved, target)` under `emit_deps`:
- numbered through `map`, so a changed mark is rewritten under the
  **same** number;
- new objects numbered from `xref.size`;
- in the base's xref kind;
- with `/Prev` at the real `start`.

**`present`: undo across a pruning save.** Delete pages, Rewrite
(`keep_only` prunes their objects), undo, save. The emission references
base objects absent from the saved file, so the planner sees
`BaseObjectsMissing` and the save is a Rewrite. The check costs one bit
test per base reference in the emitted closure.

**Root ownership: one static table per root object,** generated from the
registry.
- **Owned keys are always emitted as a function of the target state,
  never taken from `roots`:**
  - the Catalog's `/Pages`, `/Outlines`, `/PageLabels`, `/AcroForm`,
    `/SheafGenerators` and `/Version` (§11.7);
  - the AcroForm's `/Fields`, `/CO`, `/DA`, `/Q`, `/DR`,
    `/NeedAppearances` and `/SigFlags`;
  - the Outlines root's `/First`, `/Last` and `/Count`.
- **Unowned keys come from `roots`, the values actually written.** Keys
  `keep_only` removed (an `/OpenAction` to a deleted page, a pruned
  `/Names` entry) and keys a save transform dropped (`/XFA`,
  `/Perms /UR3`) stay gone.
- **Ownership is static.** A key never becomes owned only while patched,
  so set → Append → undo → Append → reopen equals the original for every
  owned key (tested per key).
- **On Rewrite,** unowned keys come from the source and are pruned by
  `keep_only` against the target page set.

**Save As is a Save.** It goes through plan, ask, materialize and
`record_save`, and its record becomes the saved file.
- **Unsigned documents** are Rewritten (`OtherTarget`). With stable
  numbering, `Src(primary, n)` objects sit at `n` in the new file.
- **Documents with any signature** use `Append { onto: CopyOf }`
  whenever the other Append conditions hold (proposal 32). The saved
  file's bytes are reflinked, or stream-copied and verified against
  `content_hash`, to the new path, and the section is appended there.
  The new record is the old one with the new path and stamp. Signatures
  survive Save As, as they do in Acrobat.

**Older records** are reduced to `{ path, at, strategy }` for the
History panel. Only the saved version's record is kept whole.

**The cost.** Sources referenced by the current state stay alive for the
session. Opening a huge file, deleting most of it and continuing keeps
its bytes until the document is closed. (M14's Save Optimized writes a
copy and reopens it as a new session; see §20.)

#### 9.2 Strategy

```rust
pub struct SavePlan { pub strategy: Strategy, pub asks: Vec<Ask>, pub transforms: Vec<SaveTransform>, pub notes: Vec<Note> }
pub enum Strategy { Unchanged, Append { onto: Onto }, Rewrite { why: Vec<Why> } }
pub enum Onto { Saved, CopyOf }       // CopyOf: Save As of a signed document — copy the saved file, then append
pub enum Why { OtherTarget, NoSaveRecord, BaseUnsound, Removes(Vec<Removed>), ReplacesText(Vec<Removed>),
               BaseObjectsMissing, TransformNeedsRewrite(SaveTransformKind), RemovalCheck { key: ObjKey },
               RecoveredRemovals /* §11.10 */, LegacyHeaderFooter /* transitional until slice 17 */ }
pub enum Ask {
    FileChanged { path: Arc<Path> },                                   // choices: Save As…, Overwrite, Cancel
    Signatures { impact: Vec<SigImpact>, append_anyway: Option<AppendAnyway>, reason: Option<Why> },
    XfaHoldsValues { fields: usize },                                  // dropping XFA that holds values
}
pub struct AppendAnyway { pub stays_readable: Vec<Removed> }          // what the earlier revision keeps
```

**Append holds only when all of these are true.** Otherwise the save is
a **Rewrite**, with reasons.
- **The target** is the saved file, or (`Onto::CopyOf`) a new path being
  written by Save As of a document that has a signature.
- **The file.** The target's `FileStamp` (§9.1) still matches. If it
  doesn't, nothing is written and the plan asks `FileChanged` (proposal
  33).
- **The base** is sound.
- **The path's effects** include neither `REMOVES` nor `REPLACES_TEXT`.
  `LIVE_REDRAW` is fine.
- **Base objects.** Every base object the emission closure references is
  present in the saved file (`SaveRecord.present`).
- **Transforms.** No pending save transform needs a rewrite (§11.8).
- **Encryption** hasn't changed (M11's first producer).
- **A save record exists.** An untitled or recovered-without-record
  document has none: `NoSaveRecord`.

**The strategy is decided before a byte is written.** Two re-checks
still happen at write time:
- The file stamp is re-checked immediately before the rename or the
  in-place append. A mismatch aborts to `FileChanged`.
- The removal check (§11.9) runs on the finished section. A veto aborts:
  - on a document with any known signature, to
    `Ask::Signatures { reason: RemovalCheck }`, which offers Rewrite or
    Cancel;
  - otherwise, the save becomes a Rewrite.

**Strict removal** (proposal 9). Deleted and replaced text never survive
in the bytes. A regenerated page number is the one exemption. Geometry
and style changes append.

**"Append anyway"** is offered on a document with signatures whenever
every Rewrite reason is one of:
- `Removes`;
- `ReplacesText`;
- `TransformNeedsRewrite(DropXfa | DropUr3 | FixSigFlags)`;
- `RemovalCheck`;
- `RecoveredRemovals`.

In that mode the emitter applies the save transforms **inside the
appended section**, as M10 slice 5's `Mode::Append` does today, and
`stays_readable` names what the earlier revision keeps: removed pages
and comments, replaced values, and the XFA datasets. It is never offered
for `BaseUnsound`, `BaseObjectsMissing`, `FileChanged` or
`NoSaveRecord`.

#### 9.3 Signatures

`SigImpact` is computed per signature, from `Source::signatures()` of
the saved file's base, against the path's `SigClass` set. M12 adds
signatures recorded in `SaveRecord`s.

| Pending | P=1 | P=2 (allows FILL) | P=3 (allows FILL \| ANNOT) | Approval signature, no MDP |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rewrite | invalid: ask | invalid: ask | invalid: ask | invalid: ask |
| Append ⊆ allowed, unlocked fields | invalid: ask | allowed | allowed | allowed, noted |
| Append ⊄ allowed | invalid: ask | invalid: ask | invalid: ask | bytes valid; ask (viewers flag it) |

- **FieldMDP** evaluates `/Action /All`, `/Include` and `/Exclude` with
  `/Fields`, and PDF 2.0's `/Lock /P`, against the `Field` keys touched.
- **A save transform in an Append that edits the Catalog or AcroForm**
  adds OTHER to the Append's class: `DropXfa`, `DropUr3`, `FixSigFlags`,
  `FillAppearances`, and a Catalog `/Version` bump (§11.7). A certified
  file therefore asks.
- **The mapping** follows ISO 32000-1 Table 254 and ISO 32000-2 Table 257.
  It stays conservative until Acrobat cross-checks it (Asks).
- **Preview.** `Tx::preview` reports DocMDP consequences per op ("this
  breaks 1 signature when saved"), so an approval card or dialog says so
  before the save. Refusing locked fields at lowering arrives with the
  first field-editing ops (M10 slice 9).

### 10. Rendering edited pages

#### 10.1 The realizer

The **realizer** turns a page of the model into something PDFium can
render: the base engine for an untouched page, or an overlay for an
edited one.

```rust
pub struct ViewInputs {
    pub marks: MarkDraw,                          // ByHost (GPUI draws sheaf marks; PDFium must not) | ByEngine | Off
    pub generators: bool,                         // false for analysis (OCR, Trim White, blank detection)
    pub ocr_over: bool,                           // draw OCR text layers (they're invisible; matters to text reads)
    pub widgets: bool,
    pub hidden: Arc<im::HashSet<AnnotId>>,        // comment filter, Hide Comments
    pub dragging: Arc<im::HashSet<AnnotId>>,
    pub ocg_off: Option<Arc<OcgOverride>>,        // layers panel: one catalog-level patch per source
    pub form_draw: FormDraw,                      // Appearances | Live (FFLDraw) until M10 decides
}
impl ViewInputs {
    pub fn screen() -> Self;
    pub fn print(opts: &PrintOpts) -> Self;
    pub fn content_only() -> Self;                // base + filters + static under-layers over the whole MediaBox:
}                                                 // no generators, marks, widgets or OCR; its content reads Page(p) only
pub struct GroupKey { pub source: SourceId, pub chunk: u32 /* base index / GROUP */ }   // GROUP from slice 2's spike
pub struct PageView { pub engine: EngineKey, pub index: PageIndex, pub key: RenderKey, pub job: JobXform }
impl Realizer {
    pub fn view(&self, st: &DocState, page: PageId, inputs: &ViewInputs) -> Result<PageView>;
}
```

**Geometry-only pages need no overlay** if spike C passes (slice 2). A
page whose only change is its `Geom` renders from the base engine with a
job transform:
- the rotation delta;
- a matrix plus a clip, for a visible box inside the base's visible box.

A crop, a rotate or a whole-document crop of 7,000 pages then opens no
group engines. A geometry change that needs pixels outside the base's
box realizes like any other edit.

**An overlay is an in-memory append section over the source's own
bytes.** The same `emit` as saves produces it, with
`EmitScope::Pages(group pages)` and `RotatePolicy::KeepBase`: the
overlay page keeps the base's `/Rotate` and the job carries the delta,
so nothing is rotated twice. The same append serializer writes it.
- **Edited pages are replaced in place** under their own object numbers.
  Their page index, AcroForm, OCProperties, inherited attributes and
  shared resources keep their context.
- **Duplicates (`copy ≥ 1`)** are appended as extra page objects under
  one new kid of the root `/Pages`, inside their original's group
  section, with inheritable keys written explicitly (§11.6).

**Overlay assembly.**
- **One allocator per source,** session-wide, maps an `ObjKey` to a
  number above the source's `/Size`. A number is assigned on first
  emission and never reused.
- **Per-page deltas are cached by `RenderKey` as symbolic objects:**
  unencrypted, with shared keys (`Res`, `GenForm`, `Shared`, `PageRes`,
  the root `/Pages`, AcroForm) left as references.
- **A group's section** is the deduplicated union of its pages' deltas,
  plus one root `/Pages` and one AcroForm built for the group, plus a
  small xref. It is encrypted **at assembly** with the source's `Crypt`,
  since RC4 and AES-128 keys depend on the final number.
- **Cost.** An edit reopens only its group's engine, about 2 ms at 7,000
  pages (measured). Other pages keep their engines and warm caches.

**Other bases and patches:**
- **Untouched pages** render from the source's base engine.
- **A source-level view patch** (OCG visibility) is one catalog-only
  overlay that serves every untouched page of that source with one
  engine.
- **Loading** uses `FPDF_LoadCustomDocument` (pdfium-render's
  `load_pdf_from_reader`) over two segments, `[base Arc, section Arc]`,
  with no O(file) copy.
- **Encrypted bases (spike A).** Sections are encrypted with the source
  key. If PDFium rejects that, the fallback, for that source only, is a
  decrypted in-memory image, built lazily and dropped with the source.
  Nothing reaches disk.
- **Damaged bases, and any base class spike B fails,** realize as a
  standalone in-memory PDF per group.

**Rejected:**
- PDFium's `GenerateContent`: lossy, about 95 ms per change, and it
  mutates the live document.
- Per-page scratch PDFs.
- Per-source generations.
- Per-page overlays.

#### 10.2 Render keys

```rust
pub struct RenderKey(u128);  // xxh3 over: base (source, index); PageDef digest minus `copy`; Geom digest minus rotate
                             // (box-only edits rendered by job transform contribute their box instead); digests of the
                             // annotations the realization DRAWS under these inputs (others' annotations, widgets; sheaf
                             // marks only under ByEngine); widget and field digests on the page (+ FormDoc digest if
                             // any widget); per covering generator: (id, recipe digest, the PageCtx fields it needs());
                             // the ViewInputs digest restricted to what the realization draws on this page
```

- Digests are cached per Arc (`OnceLock<u128>`), so a key costs
  O(annotations on the page) hash combines.
- **What it keys:** tiles by `(RenderKey, Job)`, previews by
  `(RenderKey, width, rotation)`, chars and search hits by `RenderKey`.
- **Consequences:**
  - Duplicates share tiles, and an undo back to an identical Arc hits
    the cache.
  - A content edit that keeps the source changes the key.
  - A save changes nothing, so tiles survive.
  - Dragging a sheaf mark under `ByHost` changes no key.
  - A reorder changes only the keys of pages covered by generators whose
    tokens need position or count.
- **Deleted:** `touched()`'s per-edit layout clear, `drop_stale_images`
  and the global `invalidate_tiles`. Layout rebuilds only on
  `ChangeSet.order` or `geometry`, from the first changed position.

#### 10.3 The engine pool

- **One worker thread** in sheaf-render owns `EngineKey → PdfiumEngine`,
  where
  `EngineKey = Base(SourceId) | Group(GroupKey, section digest) | SourcePatch(SourceId, digest)`.
- **Budgets.**
  - Engines serving visible pages are pinned.
  - Others are LRU, capped by count and by bytes (PDFium's RSS per
    engine, measured in slice 2).
  - **Background consumers** (OCR, Trim White, plugin page scans) have
    their own small LRU budget, so they can't evict the screen's
    engines.
- **Request semantics are kept:**
  - priorities: 0 for previews, 1 for tiles, 2–3 for prefetch, `u8::MAX`
    for background;
  - coalescing by `(RenderKey, Job)`;
  - a dropped receiver skips its render;
  - size walks and search are chunked between renders.
- **Liveness.** The pool holds the bytes `Arc` for open engines and a
  `Weak` to the `Source`, and closes engines whose source is gone.
- **Deleted:** `RenderHandle`'s thread per source,
  `Renderer::hide_annots` and `set_annots_hidden`, and their replays
  (docview.rs:298, :1722, :1901; layers_panel.rs:110;
  comments_panel.rs:621).

#### 10.4 View inputs and marks on screen

- **Hiding is data.** A hidden annotation becomes `/F |= Hidden` in the
  overlay's copy of the dict, or is left out of the realized `/Annots`.
- **Each consumer passes its own inputs:**
  - the screen passes its filters;
  - print preview passes its options;
  - OCR, Trim White and blank detection pass `content_only()`, so an
    unsaved header or watermark is never read as page content;
  - plugin renders pass `ByEngine`, with an optional "without markup".
- **Sheaf marks stay drawn by GPUI in M10.5,** with `MarkDraw::ByHost`
  telling the realizer to leave them out. The switch to PDFium drawing
  their `/AP` lands in M10 slice 12. It deletes the GPUI mark painter
  (`annot_elements`, docview.rs:5329), `stamp_preview.rs` and the tape
  pictures.
- **Header/footer, watermarks, OCR and changed fields are drawn by
  PDFium** from the realization. The GPUI header/footer overlay
  (docview.rs:5196) is deleted, so an unsaved header now shows under
  view rotation too.

#### 10.5 The text service

```rust
pub trait TextService {
    fn chars(&self, snap: &Snapshot, page: PageId, o: TextOpts) -> Result<Arc<PageChars>>;  // PageSpace boxes, 1/16 pt
    fn search(&self, snap: &Snapshot, q: &Query, pages: PageSel, o: TextOpts, p: &Progress) -> Hits;
}
pub struct TextOpts { pub fields: bool /* merge widget display text (M10 slice 14); false for plugins until 0.5 */ }
```

- **Where text comes from.** Untouched pages are read from the base
  engine and cached per `(source, index)`. Edited pages are read from
  their realization and cached per `RenderKey`. The screen, the file and
  the text agree by construction.
- **Callers:** Find, Patterns, selection, calculator picks, plugin text
  and blank detection. They walk `DocState` order in `SEARCH_CHUNK`
  steps between renders.
- **What becomes searchable:** inserted, blank, cropped, duplicated and
  unsaved-OCR pages (M11 bug 6).
- **Deleted:**
  - `layer_hits`, `merge_source_hits` and `index_of_source_page`;
  - `CharsKey::{Engine, Layer}` and `PageChars::from_layer`;
  - `text_layer::search` and `pattern_search` as a parallel path;
  - the plugin API's OCR special cases.

#### 10.6 Nothing on disk

Every temporary file goes:
- the layers panel's decrypted temp copy, which becomes a view input;
- the recovery temp primary, which is now opened from memory;
- the print temp file: bytes go to GDI from memory, and CUPS gets `lp -`
  on stdin;
- the export temp file;
- stamp and tape pictures, which are rendered from a one-annotation
  in-memory document;
- the generated cache.

The cache directory holds no document content. Besides the user's files,
sheaf writes only the recovery sidecar, the short-lived append journal
and short-lived same-directory temp files for atomic writes (§11.7).
Outside document content, it also writes the plugin installer's unpack
folder and the instance socket. An attachment the user opens in an
external app is the one user-requested file, and M11 slice 1 deletes it
at close.

### 11. Materialization

**Materialization** turns a `DocState` into bytes: a full file, an
appended section, a recovery section or an overlay.

#### 11.1 Stages

```rust
pub fn plan(input: &SaveInput, target: &SaveTarget) -> SavePlan;                    // §9.2: before any write
pub fn emit(st: &DocState, scope: EmitScope, base: &EmitBase, p: &Progress) -> Emission;   // the shared heart (pure)
pub fn rewrite_graph(e: &Emission, primary: &Src) -> Graph;    // primary loaded eagerly + emitted objects at stable numbers
pub fn serialize(e: Emission, g: Option<Graph>, how: How, prof: &Profile, p: &Progress) -> Result<Written>;
pub fn materialize(input: &SaveInput, plan: &SavePlan, target: SaveTarget, p: &Progress) -> Result<Output, SaveStop>;
pub struct Output { pub bytes: Written /* file path or in-memory bytes */, pub record: SaveRecord, pub notes: Vec<Note> }
pub enum SaveStop { Ask(Ask) /* FileChanged at write time; RemovalCheck veto on a signed document */, Failed(Error) }
pub enum EmitScope { All, Keys(Arc<ObjKeySet>) /* Append, recovery: closure under emit_deps */, Pages(Arc<[PageId]>) }
```

**The Rewrite path:**
1. emit;
2. build the graph: the primary loaded eagerly as a lopdf `Document`,
   plus the emitted objects at their stable numbers;
3. run `kept::keep_only` **unchanged**, except that it also returns the
   ids it modified;
4. apply `rules::plan`'s transforms;
5. serialize: objects neither emitted nor modified are raw-copied when
   the §11.6 conditions hold, and everything else is re-serialized.

`keep_only` is ported to a symbolic graph only if slice 9's Rewrite gate
fails on the heavy fixture.

**Bodies are produced while writing.** Built bodies that are large and
derived are `SymObject::Deferred`: generator blocks, appearance streams,
OCR streams and filtered streams. They are produced inside `serialize`,
one object at a time, written and dropped. Peak memory then stays near
the largest object plus `keep_only`'s structural graph, not the sum of
all derived bytes (gated, see Tests, fixtures and gates).

#### 11.2 Module layout: save.rs and incremental.rs taken apart

| Module | Holds | From |
|---|---|---|
| `materialize/plan.rs` | `SavePlan`, strategy, asks, `emit_deps` | save.rs `save_to_path_noting`, `signatures_broken` |
| `materialize/emit/{pages, content, annots, form, outline, catalog}.rs` | the emitter | `build_rewrite`'s loop, `write_page_annots`, `link_connectors`, `write_outline`, forms/merge.rs |
| `materialize/copy.rs` | cross-source closure copy | `copy_object`, `remap`, `is_page_node`, `inherited_attrs` |
| `materialize/graph.rs` | the Rewrite graph | `LoadedSource`, `file::load_source` |
| `materialize/verify.rs` | post-conditions (§11.9) | new |
| `kept.rs` | `keep_only`, unchanged, returning the ids it modified | — |
| `serialize/{object, xref, append, full, crypt, durable}.rs` | one writer | incremental.rs:478–550 (section framing) and :591–683 (`write_object`, `write_dict`, `write_name`), `write_atomic` + fsync |
| `export/{run, presets, transforms}.rs` | §12 | `subset`, `print_pdf`, `flatten_annots`, `export_*` |
| `content/` | §13 | stamps, watermark, text_layer, annotation installers |
| `recovery.rs` | v2 | |

Tests move with their code (save.rs :1736–5061) and are ported to ops.

#### 11.3 The emitter: objects derived from the definition

```rust
pub enum ObjKey {
    Catalog, PagesNode(u32), OutlinesRoot, AcroForm, Info, Names(NameTree), Generators /* /SheafGenerators */,
    Page(PageId),                                // a page dict (copy 0 is written at its own number)
    Base { src: SourceId, obj: ObjectId, copy: u32 },  // a source object (copied when not primary or copy ≥ 1)
    Annot(AnnotId), AnnotPart(AnnotId, Part /* Ap(state) | Companion | Popup | FileSpec */),
    Field(FieldId),                              // a merged widget's ObjKey::Annot ALIASES its terminal's Field key
    Bookmark(OutlineId), Labels,
    Block(u128 /* layout digest */),             // generator, OCR and flattened blocks; identical blocks share
    GenForm(u128), Filtered { stream: ObjRef, filter: u64 },
    PageRes { base_res: Option<ObjRef>, dropped: u64, added: u64 },
    Res(ResId),                                  // content-addressed font, image, form, ExtGState, property list
    Shared(SharedKind /* QOpen | QClose(n) | GlyphlessFont */),
}
pub struct Emission { pub objects: Vec<(ObjKey, SymObject)>, pub version: PdfVersion /* required, §11.7 */,
                      pub removed_annots: Vec<ObjRef>, pub allowed_drops: Vec<ObjKey> /* live redraws */ }
pub enum SymObject { Raw(ObjRef), Built(SymValue) /* refs are ObjKeys */, Deferred(DeferredBody) }
```

**Pages.** `emit/pages.rs` starts from the base page dict, read lazily,
and keeps unknown keys. Then:
- **Boxes and rotation.** `Geom` boxes and `/Rotate` are written only
  where they differ from what the page will inherit in its written
  position.
- **Inheritance.** When a page dict is placed under a node other than
  its source parent chain (Append inserts, duplicates, moves between
  nodes, overlays), all four inheritable keys are written explicitly
  from the page's effective values: `/Resources`, `/MediaBox`,
  `/CropBox` when it differs, and `/Rotate`, written even when it is 0.
- **Contents and resources.** `/Contents` per §11.4, `/Resources` per
  §11.5.
- **`/StructParents`** is kept only on copy 0 of a page whose source
  structure tree is written (the primary's). It is dropped on copies and
  on pages inserted from other files, and `/StructParent` is dropped
  from their annotations, with a Note when the source was tagged. M14
  models structure.
- **`/Annots`** comes from `page_annots`:
  - marks through their derived appearances, with source keys kept
    (§3.6);
  - others' annotations as the source dict, references remapped,
    copy-on-write;
  - widgets the same way. A merged widget is its terminal's one object.
  - **Links.** Links among copied pages are remapped. Links whose target
    page isn't written are dropped, by both strategies; `emit_deps`
    keeps this right across Appends.
- **`/Thumb`** is dropped whenever content changed.
- **Template pages** (`/Names /Templates`) are kept raw by `keep_only`
  on Rewrite and untouched by Append. They count as target pages for
  reachability (§11.9).

**Forms.** `emit/form.rs` is forms/merge.rs generalized:
- **Field dicts** are copied with their changed keys (`/V`, `/AS`,
  `/DA`, `/T`, `/Parent`, `/Kids`, `/P`). A `copy ≥ 1` instance gets its
  own objects.
- **A merged field and widget** (ISO 32000-1 12.7.3.1) is emitted as
  **one** object under `ObjKey::Field`, with the field's and the
  widget's changes combined. Its `ObjKey::Annot` aliases that key in the
  `ObjMap`, so a fill never defines one number twice. A copy of a merged
  pair is still one object, with no `/Parent`, so it is never orphaned.
- **`/DR` and inherited keys.** `/DR` is merged with the renames decided
  at lowering. `/DA` and `/Q` are pushed down. The owned AcroForm keys
  come from `FormDoc`, and document scripts are carried over.

**Outline.** Emitted in place by provenance. Original items keep `/A`,
`/SE` and the sign of `/Count`, and only changed items are written. A
full rebuild happens only for a new tree. `write_outline`'s
always-rebuild goes.

**Labels and generators.** `/PageLabels` comes from the `LabelSpec`s,
and `/SheafGenerators` from the generators (specs only, no paths).

**Connectors.** `/Dest` cycles resolve symbolically after the page keys
exist, so `reserve_pages`' id dance goes.

**Appearances** are derived at emit time by
`appearance(mark | widget | pinned, inputs, font) -> FormRes`. The
function is pure and memoized by input digest. The font is an explicit
input, never a global. M10 slice 7's fallback font is a late-bound
`Res::Font(Fallback)`: one GID-preserving subset per materialization or
overlay.

#### 11.4 Content assembly

```
/Contents [ gen-under… static-under…  QOpen^open  base′…  QClose^close  static-over…  gen-over… ]
```

- **`base′`** is the source page's `/Contents` minus sheaf's own
  isolation streams and blocks (recognized by marker), with any filters
  applied.
  - The original streams are referenced and **never re-encoded**.
  - With a filter, a stream becomes a lexical splice (§13), written
    under a `Filtered` key: copy-on-write, so a stream shared by blank
    pages stays intact on unfiltered pages.
- **The `replaces` rule.** A generator's `replaces` becomes
  `DropMarked(Pagination { kinds, origins: {Acrobat} })` on each covered
  page at emit. It is resolved from the memoized `blocks` fact, so it
  also covers pages inserted later.
- **Isolation** is emitted only when the page has layers or generators.
  The `open` and `close` counts come from `Balance` (§3.4). `QOpen` and
  `QClose(n)` are shared, marked streams, written once per file.
- **Every sheaf block is its own stream** with a `/SheafBlock` dict.
  - Pagination content is wrapped in `/Artifact /ShP<n> BDC … EMC`, with
    a named property list (§3.5).
  - OCR blocks are untagged invisible text wrapped in
    `/ShOCR /ShP<n> BDC … EMC`. Their named property list
    `<< /SheafOcrBlock (b<hex>) >>` lets a merged stream still classify,
    and Strip still be a dict edit. The key differs from the legacy font
    name `/SheafOcr` by more than case.

**The `/SheafBlock` dict, per kind.** Every value but `/K` is a string.

| Kind | `/SheafBlock` | Inside the stream |
|---|---|---|
| Generator | `<< /K /Gen /G (g<hex>) /L <layout hex> /R <recipe hex> /N (000123) >>`; `/N` only with Bates | `/Artifact /ShP<n> BDC … EMC`, property list `<< /Type /Pagination /Subtype … /SheafGen (g<hex>) >>` |
| OCR | `<< /K /Ocr /B (b<hex>) >>`: the layer's `BlockId` | `/ShOCR /ShP<n> BDC … EMC`, property list `<< /SheafOcrBlock (b<hex>) >>` |
| Flattened | `<< /K /Flat /B (b<hex>) >>`: the layer's `BlockId` | the placements, tagged per `PlaceTag` |
| Isolation | `<< /K /Isolate >>` | `q`s (`QOpen`) or `Q`s (`QClose(n)`) |

#### 11.5 Copy-on-write and resources

**The writer never writes a source object's number with different
content,** except in two cases:
- **Model-owned identities:**
  - page dicts (copy 0);
  - annotation, field and outline dicts that came from the file;
  - the Catalog, `/Pages` nodes, AcroForm and Info.
- **Rewrite pruning:** objects `keep_only` edits in place, such as
  structure elements' `/K`, `/ParentTree` nodes and `/Names` subtrees.

`verify.rs` enforces this as a post-condition (§11.9): it compares each
written source number's content against the source, unless its `ObjKey`
is model-owned or `keep_only` reported it.

**Shared objects are never modified in place.**
- Shared streams, Form XObjects, images, fonts and Resources dicts are
  left alone.
- A transformed stream is a new key, memoized by
  `(source object, transform)`.
- "All uses" edits are M13 and M14 work, which adds `Source::users` and
  an in-model users index with its first producer.

**Pages get a `PageRes`,** a new dict built from the base page's
resources.
- **One level down.** It copies the base's entries one level down in
  each category it changes.
- **Added names** are collision-free: `/Sh<base32(ResId)[..6]>`, bumped
  if the base already uses the name.
- **Dropped names.** After filters, the names still used by the
  remaining content are computed (`content/names`). Names used **only**
  inside dropped ranges are omitted. The Form XObject an Acrobat
  watermark draws, and its ExtGState and fonts, are then no longer
  reachable from that page. The unlinked objects go to the removal
  check.
- **Sharing.** Pages that share one base Resources dict, one
  dropped-name set and one added-name set share one `PageRes`. A
  watermark over 7,000 pages that share a Resources dict therefore
  writes **one** Resources dict, one font, one ExtGState and one image.

**Cross-source copy** never follows into a page it isn't copying; that
reference becomes Null. Links among copied pages are kept. Copied
annotations and fields are private to their copy.

#### 11.6 Numbering, raw passthrough, object streams, the page tree

**Rewrite numbering is stable,** for in-place saves and Save As:
- every surviving primary object keeps its number, and removed ones
  become free entries;
- keys already in the previous `SaveRecord.map` keep their numbers;
- new keys are numbered from `size` in a deterministic order: pages in
  order, then annotations by id, fields by id, resources by digest;
- `renumber_objects` is no longer used.

**Raw passthrough.** An object is copied as its original bytes only when
**all** of these hold. Everything else is re-serialized and re-encrypted
under its final `(num, gen)`.
1. The numbering is `Stable`.
2. The object comes from the primary source.
3. The target's `Crypt` equals the source's: the same algorithm, file
   key and `/ID[0]`. For R ≤ 4 the per-object key depends on number and
   generation.
4. The object is untouched by emit and by `keep_only`.
5. The object isn't an object-stream member, and its xref generation
   equals its `n g obj` header.

Under these conditions, a Rewrite at 7,000 pages is mostly memcpy plus
`keep_only`'s structural parse.

**Object streams.**
- Object-stream containers are never raw-copied.
- Their surviving members are re-serialized as **plain objects**,
  encrypted individually when the target is encrypted. That matches
  today, so it is no regression.
- M10.5 writes no object streams, in Rewrite or Append. Packing is M14's
  (§20, proposal 37), with ISO 32000-1 7.5.7's exclusions and the rule
  that strings inside an object stream are not encrypted separately.

**Dense numbering for exports,** with byte-identical stream
deduplication.

**The page tree.**
- **Append** keeps the saved file's `TreeShape` and rewrites changed
  `/Pages` nodes **in place** under their own numbers.
  - A page stays in its node, and inserted pages join their
    predecessor's node.
  - A node splits only once it grows past max(128, its size at open), so
    a flat 7,000-kid tree stays flat.
  - Only nodes whose `/Kids` or `/Count` changed are written, plus the
    page dicts that changed node (with explicit inheritable keys).
  - A reorder in a flat tree writes one object.
- **Rewrite** writes a balanced tree with fanout 32.

#### 11.7 The serializer, encryption and durability

- **`serialize/object.rs`** is the hand-written object writer (lopdf's
  Writer is private). Reals are written in shortest round-trip form. A
  non-finite real is refused, and magnitudes are clamped to ±3.4e38
  (Annex C).
- **`serialize/xref.rs`:**
  - **Full writes** produce one subsection `[0, Size)` with free entries
    linked in a list, or an xref stream with `/Index` when the base used
    xref streams.
  - **Appends** write runs. An object deleted in an Append becomes a
    free entry with generation + 1.
  - **The header offset.** Appended xref offsets are written relative to
    the header offset `h` recorded in `XrefFacts` (position − h), so
    files with leading junk work in PDFium, pdf.js and qpdf.
- **`serialize/append.rs`.** An Append never uses a `Source`'s bytes as
  its base. Its base is **the saved file on disk**, whose identity is
  the `SaveRecord`.
  - **In place.** The section is assembled in memory (Appends are
    small), and the removal check (§11.9) runs on it before any byte
    reaches the file. Then: re-check the stamp; write a journal
    `<name>.pdf.sheaf-append` beside the recovery sidecar, holding
    `{ pre_len, stamp }`, and fsync it and the directory; append the
    section to the file (in the base's xref kind, `/Prev` at the real
    start, a stale `/XRefStm` ignored, `/ID[0]` kept and `/ID[1]`
    updated, `/Encrypt` repeated in the trailer); fsync; remove the
    journal.
  - **A torn tail.** On open, a journal whose file is longer than
    `pre_len` and doesn't end in a complete section is our own torn
    write. The file is truncated back to `pre_len`, with a notice.
  - **`Onto::CopyOf`** reflinks (FICLONE) where the filesystem supports
    it, or else stream-copies and verifies against `content_hash`, to a
    temp file in the target directory. It appends there and renames.
  - **Empty sections.** Nothing changed means nothing is written.
- **`serialize/full.rs`** writes no `/Prev` or `/XRefStm`.
- **`serialize/crypt.rs`** encrypts every written string and stream with
  the target's `Crypt` through `encrypt_object`, including strings in
  stream dicts (lopdf #603).
  - It honors `/EFF` for embedded files.
  - It skips `/Encrypt`, xref streams, and `/Metadata` when
    EncryptMetadata is false.
  - **IVs.** The AES-CBC IV is the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA256(file
    key, ObjKey ‖ plaintext). Output stays deterministic, and no IV is
    reused for different plaintexts of one object across saves.
- **Encryption by profile.**
  - `Profile.encryption = Keep` is the default for saves, Save As and
    recovery.
  - `None` is for print and page images.
  - New encryption is M11's first producer.
  - Loads stop stripping `/Encrypt`, which was the root of M11 bug 2.
- **The PDF version.** The emitter accumulates the version its features
  need: ExtGState `/ca` 1.4, xref streams 1.5, AES 1.6, artifact
  subtypes 1.7.
  - A Rewrite writes the header at that version.
  - An Append sets Catalog `/Version` only when it is higher. That is a
    Catalog change, so it adds OTHER (§9.3).
- **`serialize/durable.rs`** writes a full file to a unique temp name
  (`.<name>.<random>.sheaf-tmp`) in the target directory, preserving
  permissions. It fsyncs the file, re-checks the target's stamp
  (aborting to `FileChanged`), renames, and fsyncs the directory.
- **Determinism.** The same `(state, sources, previous map, profile)`
  gives the same bytes. Only ordered maps are used in emission, and the
  IV rule is deterministic.

#### 11.8 Save-time form transforms: policy, not history

`forms/rules.rs` is rewritten as a pure function run at plan time:

```rust
pub fn plan(base: &SourceForm, target: &DocState /* FormDoc + nodes */, path: &PathEffects,
            rec: &SaveRecord, strategy: &Strategy) -> Vec<SaveTransform>;
pub enum SaveTransform { DropXfa { tooltips_to_tu: bool }, DropUr3, FixSigFlags, FillAppearances }
```

- **`DropXfa`** applies when the field tree or values differ from the
  base (after `XfaHoldsValues`, when the datasets hold values).
  - Tooltips are copied onto the **target's** fields, inserted forms
    included.
  - It rides an Append when the XFA datasets hold no value the target
    has replaced. Otherwise it is `TransformNeedsRewrite(DropXfa)`, so
    the old values don't survive in the earlier revision.
- **`DropUr3`** is decided per strategy (proposal 34).
  - **On a Rewrite** it always applies, because UR3's signature covers
    the original bytes.
  - **On an Append** it applies only when the path's `SigClass` isn't
    within UR3's `/TransformParams`: `/Form [/FillIn …]` allows FILL,
    and `/Annots [/Create /Delete /Modify …]` allows ANNOT. Reader
    validates later revisions against those, so a permitted fill or
    comment keeps Reader's rights.
- **`FixSigFlags`** applies only alongside a UR removal, as today. It
  decides from the signed terminals and certification in the **target**
  (signed fields brought in from other files count), never from the
  primary's form alone.
- **`FillAppearances`** creates appearances for valued fields in a
  NeedAppearances file (M10's producer).

**Who applies the transforms.** The emitter applies them to the Catalog
and AcroForm emission on every path: Rewrite, Append (allowed or
"anyway"), recovery sections (§11.10) and exports (§12).
- `SaveRecord.transforms` and `roots` remember what the file already
  lost, so an Append never repeats a drop.
- Undo across such a save restores nothing in the file, because the
  model never held the change. The next save re-applies the policy
  against the base, idempotently.

#### 11.9 Post-conditions on every output: fail closed

**1. The removal check** runs on every Append, and as a test oracle on
every Rewrite.
- **What it examines.** It looks at each object the section overwrites,
  and each subgraph that becomes unlinked. A subgraph is unlinked when
  its references are no longer present in the new versions of the
  objects that held them. Whether some other, untouched object still
  references a target is unknown, so it counts as unlinked: the check
  errs toward a Rewrite.
- **The old side is the saved file itself.** The check opens the target
  through `LazyPdf` over positional reads after the stamp check, and
  reads only the objects it needs.
- **Derived objects are judged on their record inputs.** These are the
  objects sheaf derives: mark and widget appearances, generator blocks,
  OCR blocks, isolation streams and `PageRes`. The check compares their
  text inputs between the saved state and the target: contents, `/RC`,
  struck text, tape rows, widget value and display, recipe text, OCR
  words. This is exact, and needs no font decoding.
- **Objects from a source are judged on decoded text.** For them the
  check builds a `TextBag` from:
  - string objects;
  - content and appearance string operands, decoded through each font's
    encoding or `/ToUnicode` to NFC, as whitespace-normalized word
    multisets;
  - every stream reachable from the unlinked or overwritten objects:
    Form XObjects, CharProcs, `/RC`, JavaScript, XFA, XMP;
  - hashes of image and embedded-file streams.

  An unknown encoding hashes the raw bytes and counts as differing.
- **GID-preserving subsets.** Every font sheaf embeds uses
  `CIDToGIDMap /Identity` with CID = original GID, so encoded strings
  depend only on font and text, and re-subsetting never looks like a
  removal.
- **A veto.** Anything in the old bag that is missing from the new one,
  and not in `Emission.allowed_drops` (live redraws whose `/R` is
  unchanged), vetoes the Append.
  - On a document with any known signature, the save stops at
    `Ask::Signatures { reason: RemovalCheck }`.
  - Otherwise it becomes a Rewrite.
  - `RemovalMisclassified { key }`, an internal diagnostic and not an
    `EditError`, is logged and counted. The corpus run asserts the count
    is zero for derived objects, and reports vetoes on source objects
    for tuning.
- **"Append anyway".** When the user chose it, the check reports instead
  of vetoing.

**2. Reachability.**
- Every reference resolves.
- The target pages are the page tree plus the pages reachable only
  through the written `/Names /Templates`; no other page is reachable.
- Every `Res` and blob is present.

**3. Forms.** `forms::check` reports no finding the input didn't already
have.

**4. Private data.** With `Private::Strip`, no `private::KEYS` name
appears (§15).

**5. Encryption.** In debug builds, with `Keep`, every string and stream
is encrypted.

**6. Copy-on-write** (§11.5).

#### 11.10 Recovery

**Sidecar v2.** The format is:

```
sheaf-recovery 2\n<append|full>\n<base len>\n<base stamp hex>\n<flags>\n<bytes>
```

- **Append sections** come from the same append serializer, from the
  saved record to the cursor, with `Profile::Recovery`.
  - **No strategy rules.** A section may "remove" by omission, since it
    is never the final file.
  - **Form transforms do apply** (`rules::plan`), so a crash-recovered
    fill of a hybrid form has no stale XFA.
- **Full** is used when base objects are missing, the base is unsound,
  or there is no save record.
- **Flags.** `removes` is set when the saved → cursor path the section
  carries has `REMOVES` or `REPLACES_TEXT`, or a transform that needs a
  Rewrite.
- **Encryption.** The sidecar is encrypted for encrypted documents, so
  owner-only and password files get recovery, and no plaintext is
  written.

**The lifecycle.**
- **Autosave** runs 3 s after the last edit and at least every 30 s. It
  is skipped while the cursor's version token is the one the last
  sidecar wrote, and it waits for an in-flight save.
- **`record_save`** synchronously writes a new sidecar against the new
  record when the cursor differs from the saved version, and deletes the
  sidecar otherwise. A stale sidecar never outlives its base.
- **Restoring** verifies the base's length and stamp. It opens
  `disk base + sections` from memory as a `Memory(Recovered)` source.
  Its synthesized record has the original path and
  `pending_tail = { bytes: sections, removes }` from the sidecar's
  flags. A Full sidecar restores with no usable base for the disk file
  (`NoSaveRecord`), so its first save is a Rewrite.
  - **The first save** appends `pending_tail + new section` to the file
    in place when the tail's `removes` flag is clear, so signed files
    stay appendable.
  - **When `removes` is set,** the plan adds `Why::RecoveredRemovals`: a
    Rewrite, or on a signed document the ask with "Append anyway". A
    crash never leaves removed text readable in an earlier revision
    without asking.
  - **If the plan is a Rewrite,** the Rewrite simply replaces the file.
  - **A second crash** offers the chained sections: the recovered
    document writes its own Append sidecars against the same disk base.

### 12. Exports: transforms on an export transaction

```rust
pub struct Export { pub from: Snapshot, pub transforms: Vec<Transform>, pub profile: Profile, pub out: Out }
pub struct Profile { pub encryption: Encryption /* Keep | None */, pub numbering: Numbering /* Stable | Dense */,
                     pub private: Private /* Keep | Strip */ }
pub enum Out { File { path: PathBuf, overwrite: bool }, Bytes }      // not `Target`: that is a bookmark's (§3.8)
pub fn preflight(snap: &Snapshot, e: &Export) -> Vec<Gate>;      // Proceed | Ask | Refuse
pub fn run(e: Export, asks: &AskAnswers, p: &Progress) -> Result<Exported, ExportError>;  // the ONLY writer besides Save
```

**What `run` does:**
1. Runs the preflight gates (§12.1).
2. Runs the resolve stage, for transforms that need engines. M11's
   partial-image redaction is its first producer; none exists in M10.5.
3. Lowers the transforms on an `ExportTx` over the snapshot, with the
   same lowering, rules and validation as the binder. It uses scratch
   ids and no history.
4. Runs `rules::plan` (target against base, strategy Rewrite) and
   applies its transforms. Every way out follows the form rules.
5. Materializes with `EmitScope::All` and the profile, with progress and
   cancel, and `Deferred` bodies.
6. Verifies (§11.9).
7. Writes atomically with fsync, or returns bytes.

A failure anywhere writes nothing. Exports run as jobs with progress and
cancel, stay out of the History panel and plugin events, and are `Send`.
Generator semantics match the binder: live tokens are evaluated against
the exported document, and frozen Bates numbers keep the binder's
numbers.

#### 12.1 Preflight gates

- **Refuse the binder's own file** as a target, for every export. Today
  only Clean Copy and Export Flattened refuse; `extract_pages` and split
  don't.
- **Ask** before signatures in the exported content stop validating
  (`SigImpact` against the export's Rewrite).
- **Ask `XfaHoldsValues`** when the export drops XFA that holds values.
- **Check capability.**
- **M11** adds unapplied redactions here, as the one choke point for
  "every way out asks first".

#### 12.2 FlattenAnnotations

- It respects Print, Hidden and NoView.
- For widgets it uses `/AS` with **no first-state fallback** (M10 bug
  10). The fallback stays for non-widgets.
- It keeps links.
- It never merges or decodes streams. It adds a `Placements` layer per
  page whose forms are `FormRef::Base` (source appearances) or
  `FormRef::Pinned` (derived appearances with their inputs captured),
  and removes the annotations through the cascade rules.
- Its `removed_annots` go into `keep_only` for OBJR pruning.
- It is both a binder op and an export step, with identical semantics.

#### 12.3 Presets

| Preset | Transforms | Profile |
|---|---|---|
| Extract / Export Pages / Split part | `KeepPages { ids, Headings, labels: true }` | Keep (proposal 10), Dense |
| Print / Save as PDF | `KeepPages { Drop }` + (`FlattenAnnotations { Printable }` or `RemoveMarkup`) (+ `FlattenFields` after M10 slice 15) | None, Bytes |
| Export Flattened | `FlattenAnnotations { All }` (+ `FlattenFields`) | Keep (proposal 10) |
| Clean Copy | `RemoveMarkup { keep: links, widgets }` + `RemovePrivateData` | Keep (proposal 10), Strip |
| Page images / text | the Print transforms, realized from bytes in memory | Bytes |
| Remove Blanks (CLI) | `KeepPages(kept)` and `KeepPages(rejects)`, two exports | Keep |

**Not exports:**
- **Save As** is a Save (§9.1).
- **Combine** is a binder flow: open the first file, apply one
  transaction, then save (§14.1, §14.2).
- **Scan and images-to-PDF** are `Session::new_empty` plus inserts, then
  a save.

### 13. The content toolkit: the lexical layer, the writer and fonts

**Pulled forward from M11** into `sheaf-doc/src/content/`:

```rust
pub enum Tok<'a> { Op(&'a [u8]), Operand(&'a [u8]) /* raw text: numbers kept as written */, InlineImage(Range<usize>) }
pub fn lex<'a>(s: &'a [u8], res: &ResCx, lim: &Limits) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<(Range<usize>, Tok<'a>), LexError>>;
pub struct Marked { pub tag: Name, pub props: Option<Props>, pub span: SpanAcross, pub depth: u8 }
pub fn marked(streams: &[&[u8]], props: &PropertiesDict, lim: &Limits) -> Result<Vec<Marked>, LexError>;
pub struct Balance { pub net: i32, pub min_prefix: i32 }
pub fn balance(streams: &[&[u8]]) -> Result<Balance, LexError>;
pub fn splice(streams: &[&[u8]], drop: &[SpanAcross]) -> Result<Vec<Vec<u8>>, SpliceError>;  // byte-preserving
pub fn names_used(streams: &[&[u8]], except: &[SpanAcross]) -> NameSet;    // for PageRes pruning
pub fn text_bag(streams: &[&[u8]], fonts: &FontDecoders) -> TextBag;        // decoded, NFC, word multiset
pub struct PageBlocks { pub marked: Arc<[Marked]>, pub pagination: Arc<[(PaginationKind, PagOrigin, SpanAcross)]>,
                        pub sheaf: Arc<[SheafBlockRef]> /* by stream marker or by marked-content marker */,
                        pub isolation: Arc<[ObjRef]>, pub legacy_ocr: Option<LegacyOcr /* the q + marked 'Q…' pair */>,
                        pub balance: Balance, pub nested_in_forms: bool }
```

**Lexing.**
- **Inline images** follow PDFium's `ReadInlineStream` rules:
  - an exact byte count for unfiltered images (W × H × BPC × components
    / 8);
  - EOD markers for `/AHx` and `/A85`;
  - the EOI for DCT;
  - `/L` or `/Length` where present (PDF 2.0);
  - only then the whitespace-delimited `EI` scan.

  An ignored corpus test compares the lexer's operator boundaries with
  PDFium's page-object enumeration, per page.
- **The logical concatenation.** Lexing works on the concatenation of a
  page's `/Contents`, because a BDC may open in one stream and close in
  the next.
- **Errors.** A stream that won't lex is an error the op reports ("page
  12's content can't be read; nothing was changed"). It is never skipped
  silently.

**Splicing is safe or refused.** A dropped range is accepted only in two
cases:
- its bytes are q/Q- and BT/ET-balanced on their own;
- or it lies wholly inside one BT…ET and contains no text-state or
  positioning operators.

Otherwise it is `EditError::Content`, naming the page. Removing a
running head drawn in the middle of a text object can therefore never
shift the body text. Pixel tests check the pixels outside each removed
artifact's bbox, in slices 6 and 16.

**Classification (`PagOrigin`):**
- **`Sheaf`:** a `/SheafBlock` stream, or a `/SheafGen` property.
- **`SheafLegacy`:** `/SheafStamp true`, `SheafSettings`, `/SheafHF`,
  `/SheafOcrLayer`.
- **`Acrobat`:** the range draws only a Form XObject whose `/PieceInfo`
  has `ADBE_CompoundType` with
  `/Private /Header|/Footer|/Watermark|/Background`.
- **`Authored`:** every other `/Artifact /Pagination`. Word, LibreOffice
  and InDesign mark their running heads this way, and sheaf never
  removes these unasked.

**Removal targets top-level page streams.** Artifacts nested inside
forms are reported through `nested_in_forms` as "left in place", never
skipped silently.

**`ContentWriter`** handles q/Q, cm, colors (gray, RGB, CMYK),
ExtGState, paths, text through a `Font`, images, and `place_form`
(honoring BBox, Matrix and the stamp page's `/Rotate`). It writes BDC
with **named** property lists only, and emits symbolic `ResourceNeeds`
that the emitter names and deduplicates.

**Fonts.**

```rust
trait Font { fn encode(&self, s: &str) -> (Vec<u8>, Unencoded); fn advance(&self, b: &[u8], size: f32) -> f32;
             fn wrap(...); fn covers(&self, c: char) -> bool; fn resource(&self) -> FontRes; }
```

- **Implementations.** `Std14`: AFM widths for all 14, with exact cp1252
  (WinAnsi), Symbol and ZapfDingbats encoders. `Glyphless`: for OCR.
- **What it replaces:** four encoders and three width tables
  (`stamps::win_ansi`/`pdf_literal`, `annot::content_literal`, the
  watermark's UTF-8-into-WinAnsi bug, and the summary's wrap).
- **Embedded fonts.** Any font sheaf embeds is GID-preserving (CID =
  GID).
- **Users.** Mark appearances, header/footer, watermark, OCR text,
  flatten placement, the comment summary and tapes all use it.

**Unencodable text (interim, until M10 slice 7)** is treated the same in
marks and generators (proposal 22). Characters the font can't encode
draw `'?'` in the appearance or block only. `/Contents`, specs and
recipes keep the exact text, and a one-time notice per document says so.
M10 slice 7's Noto Type0 fallback, behind the same trait, ends it.

**Never through lopdf's `Content::decode/encode`.** Per-crate
`clippy.toml` bans land in the slice that removes the last user (Lint
ledgers). `stamps::strip`, `watermark::read` and `has_artifacts`'
decompress-every-page scan are deleted, and the memoized `blocks` fact
replaces them.

**Stays in M11:**
- the semantic interpreter: graphics and text state, glyph boxes,
  foreign font metrics, Type3;
- Form XObject instancing per use;
- clipping;
- removal transforms;
- image editing;
- descriptors.

M11's interpreter consumes `Tok` spans, so nothing here is thrown away.

### 14. Consumers

#### 14.1 The app: one door, one refresh

```rust
impl DocView {
    pub fn commit(&mut self, tx: Tx, opts: CommitOpts, cx: &mut Context<Self>) -> Result<Commit, EditError>;
    fn on_change(&mut self, ch: &ChangeSet, cx: &mut Context<Self>);     // the only refresh
}
pub struct ChangeSet {
    pub from: VersionId, pub to: VersionId,
    pub cause: Cause /* Commit | Amend | Undo | Redo | Jump | Saved | Rollback | Revert */,
    pub actor: Actor, pub depth: u8, pub keys: Arc<KeySet>,
    pub order: bool, pub pages_added: Vec<PageId>, pub pages_removed: Vec<PageId>,
    pub geometry: PageSet, pub content: PageSet /* render keys may differ */,
    pub annots: PageSet, pub fields: Vec<FieldId>, pub outline: bool, pub labels: bool, pub form: bool, pub generators: bool,
}
```

A **ChangeSet** says what one transition between versions changed, for
every consumer that has to follow it.

**One door.**
- **The three funnels become one:** `finish_edit`, the hand-written
  `touched()` + `after_model_change` pairs, and OCR's `apply_in_job`.
- **`DocView::commit` returns the error.** It shows the notice
  centrally, and plugin callers get it as `result`.
- **One refresh.** Undo, redo, jump, `record_save`, `rollback_group` and
  `revert_group` return a `ChangeSet` into the same `on_change`, which:
  - prunes selection, region, chars and text selection of removed ids;
  - relayouts only on `order` or `geometry`;
  - lets caches follow render keys;
  - schedules autosave;
  - publishes the new `Snapshot` and `LowerCx` to the document's plugin
    cell (an `ArcSwap`, §14.3);
  - emits one `DocEvent::Changed`.
- **New ids come from `Commit.created`.**

**Background planners.**
- **Concurrent short planners.** `insert_file`, blank and image inserts,
  blank review, comment import and Trim White build a `Tx` off-thread on
  a snapshot and commit with a base (policies in §8.5). They run
  concurrently with each other and with OCR.
- **OCR** keeps its own run, a long streaming producer.
- **One at a time.** `jobs::run_job`'s rule now covers only jobs that
  write files: exports and split. Saves are serialized per document by
  the seal and the second-save wait.
- The `NoProgress` paths and bare `background_executor().spawn` calls in
  page_insert.rs go.

**Dialogs** resolve indexes to ids against the version they opened on,
preview in the background, re-lower the preview on every `ChangeSet`,
and commit with `Base::At(opened)`.

**Menus** use `Tx::check`: no lowering, no ids.

**The clipboard** carries copied marks as JSON in the source page's view
frame. The blobs they reference travel in a process-wide, in-memory blob
cache keyed by `BlobId`. Paste in any window interns them into that
document's Store before lowering `AddMark`.

**Saving:**
1. `save_input(v)` seals v.
2. `plan` runs on a background thread.
3. Ask any `Ask`s.
4. `materialize` runs on a background thread.
5. `record_save`, or `abandon_save`.

Nothing is reopened, no tile is dropped, and edits stay enabled
throughout.

**Combine** builds the combined document (§14.2), saves it to the path
the dialog asked for, and hands **the same `Document`** to a new window.
Its history starts at the combined version (the first file's opened
